Calculate

\text{AIC}_{\lambda_{i}}
or
\text{BIC}_{\lambda_{i}}
using (
25
);

12:

Find
m=\arg\min_{i=1,...,n}\text{AIC}_{\lambda_{i}}
or
m=\arg\min_{i=1,...,n}\text{BIC}_{\lambda_{i}}
;

13:

end

for

13:

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\lambda_{m}}
and
\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*},\lambda_{m}}^{(k+1)}
.

6
Numerical Experiments

6.1
Simulation

We conduct simulation to investigate the performances of the proposed
methods. We generate independent target data of size
n_{\mathcal{T}}=150
from a linear
model:

y_{t}=\mu_{0}+\bm{z}_{t}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{0}+\varepsilon_{t}=\bm{x}_{t}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{0}+\varepsilon_{t},t=1,2,\ldots,n_{\mathcal{T}},

where
\mu_{0}=1
is an intercept,
\bm{\theta}_{0}
is a vector of ones with dimension

100
, and
\varepsilon_{t}\sim\mathbb{N}(0,1)
. Large external data of size

n_{\Xi}=20000
are generated from a linear model with mean-shifts:

y_{e}=\mu_{0}+\bm{z}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{0}+\gamma_{e}+\varepsilon_{e}=\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{0}+\gamma_{e}+\varepsilon_{e},e=1,2,\ldots,n_{\Xi},

where
\mu_{0}
,
\bm{\theta}_{0}
, and the distribution of
\varepsilon_{e}
are the same
as in the target data model. We use the same covariate distribution
\bm{z}
for
the target data and the external data. We consider both light-tailed
distribution
\bm{z}\sim\mathbb{N}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma})
and heavy-tailed distribution

\bm{z}\sim T_{3}(\bm{\Sigma})
, where the
i,j
-th element of
\bm{\Sigma}
is

\bm{\Sigma}_{ij}=0.5^{|i-j|}
,
1\leq i,j,\leq 100
.

We consider three distinct cases characterized by different bias term
(
\gamma_{e}
) distributions, detailed below:

1.

Case 1. Sparse Bias Terms (SP):
Only a subset
of the large dataset is biased. Specifically, 70% of the data points are
randomly selected as biased, and their bias terms are generated from

\gamma_{e}\sim 2+\Gamma(1,1)
, where

\Gamma(1,1)
represents gamma distribution with shape and scale parameters
equal to 1.

2.

Case 2. Heavy-Tailed Bias Terms (HT):
All data points in
the external data are biased, with bias terms following a heavy-tailed
distribution:
\gamma_{e}\sim|t_{2}|
. This scenario represents situations where
outliers exhibit large deviations.

3.

Case 3. High-Leverage Bias Terms (HL):

All data points are biased, with bias terms related to the leverage
scores. Specifically,

\gamma_{e}=c\xi_{e}\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{\Xi}^{\top}\bm{X}_{\Xi})^{-1}\bm{x}_{e}
, where

\xi_{e}\sim\Gamma(1,1)
and
c=n_{\Xi}/(d-1)
. This
scenario accounts for high-leverage outliers, as the bias terms are influenced
by the covariates’ leverage scores.

We repeat the simulation for
K=500
times and evaluate the performances of the
proposed methods using an empirical trimmed mean square error (eMSE). For a set
of positive values
\{v_{k}:k=1,\ldots,K\}
, the trimmed mean with trimming
proportion
\alpha
is defined as

\text{Trim}\left(\{v_{i}:k=1,\ldots K\}\right)=\frac{1}{(1-2\alpha)K}\sum_{k=\lfloor\alpha K\rfloor}^{\lfloor(1-\alpha)K\rfloor}v_{(i)},

where
\lfloor\cdot\rfloor
is the floor function and

v_{(i)},i=1,\ldots,K
are the order statistics of
v_{1},\ldots,v_{K}
.
Our eMSE is defined as

\mathrm{eMSE}(\hat{\bm{\beta}})=\text{Trim}\left(\left\{\|\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{k}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}:k=1,\ldots,K\right\}\right),

where
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{k}
is the estimate in
the
k
-th replication. We use
\alpha=0.1
for evaluations. Besides eMSE, we
also calculate the
empirical biases (squared) and empirical trimmed variances:

\mathrm{eBias}^{2}=\left\|\bar{\hat{\bm{\beta}}}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2},\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{eVar}=\text{Trim}\left(\left\{\left\|\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{k}-\bar{\hat{\bm{\beta}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}:k=1,\ldots,K\right\}\right),

respectively, where
\bar{\hat{\bm{\beta}}}=K^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{k}
.
We set the sampling
rates to be
\rho=0.0075
,
0.03
,
0.12
, and
0.48
, which correspond to
nominal sample sizes of
n_{\mathcal{T}},4n_{\mathcal{T}},16n_{\mathcal{T}}
, and
64n_{\mathcal{T}}
, respectively. For combined
estimators, equal expected number of observations are taken from the
target-guided selection and the leverage-based random sampling.

\ell_{1}
penalty

We first present results using
\ell_{1}
penalty. Figure
4
presents
the eMSE.

(a)

SP (light-tailed
\bm{x}
)

(b)

HT (light-tailed
\bm{x}
)

(c)

HL (light-tailed
\bm{x}
)

(d)

SP (heavy-tailed
\bm{x}
)

(e)

HT (heavy-tailed
\bm{x}
)

(f)

HL (heavy-tailed
\bm{x}
)

Figure 4
:
The eMSE of transfer learning estimators with
\ell_{1}
penalty. The
first row is for light-tailed
\bm{x}
and the second row is for heavy-tailed

\bm{x}
. The dashed line represents the least squares estimator
(
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}
) with only target data
\mathcal{T}
. The solid line represents the
transfer learning estimator (
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{full}}
) with full data

\mathcal{T}\cup\Xi
.

Figure
4
shows that most transfer learning estimators outperform

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}
, although negative transfer is possible. Among all cases in our
simulations, the two combined estimators perform the best, suggesting that they
benefit from both target-guided selection and optimal subsampling.

In subfigures (
4(a)
) and (
4(d)
) for Case “SP,” bias
terms are relatively large, and transfer learning estimators using full data
(
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{full}}
) and random sampling (
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{uni}}
and

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}
) show a negative transfer effect; they perform worse than

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}
. In this case,
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}
avoids negative transfer. It performs
very similarly to
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}
when the sampling rate is low and demonstrates
a more significant positive transfer effect as the subsample size from external
data increases.

For Cases “HT” and “HL,” there is no negative transfer, and
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}

performs relatively better, while
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}
may be less efficient. This is
because
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}
behaves similarly to
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}
with small external
subsamples and is subject to high variance.

Notably, the eMSE does not always decrease with larger subsample sizes, which
contrasts with the common trend in subsampling literature that larger
subsamples typically yield better performance and the full data estimator
performs the best. For instance, in subfigures (
4(a)
) and
(
4(d)
) for Case “SP”, the eMSE of
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{c(e)}}
and

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{c(d)}}
initially decreases but later increases, outperforming the
full data estimator. The random subsampling estimators also surpass the full
data estimator in this case. This observation aligns with our theoretical
insight. As discussed in Section
3
, the error bound of
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}

is not monotonically decreasing with sample size
r
, indicating a bias-variance
trade-off in data selection for transfer learning. The
decreasing-then-increasing trend is more pronounced for combined estimators, as
they are specifically designed to optimize this trade-off. This phenomenon
highlights the importance of data selection in mitigating negative transfer.

To further illustrate the bias-variance trade-off, we present the bias and
variance in Figure
5
. Here, we only present the results for
Case “HL”, which correspond to Figures
4
(
4(c)
) and

4
(
4(f)
). We leave results for other cases in the
supplementary material to save space.

2
of Figure
4
(
4(c)
)

eVar of Figure
4
(
4(c)
)

eBias
2
of Figure
4
(
4(f)
)

eVar of Figure
4
(
4(f)
)

Figure 5
:

The eBias
2
and eVar of transfer learning estimators with
\ell_{1}

penalty for Case “HL”.

From Figure
5
, we observe that the estimator
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}
has the
smallest bias among all sampling estimators, whereas random sampling generally
results in large biases, with leverage-based random sampling exhibiting the
highest bias. The biases of the two combined estimators lie between those of

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}
and
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}
, which aligns with our expectations. In terms of
variance, leverage-based subsampling results in the smallest variance, while

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}
has relatively large variance. Similarly, the variances of the
combined estimators fall between those of
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}
and
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}
. These
findings suggest that combined estimators outperform target-guided selection and
leverage sampling by better balancing bias and variance.

penalty

We now present simulations with the
\ell_{2}
penalty, using the same settings as
for
\ell_{1}
. Figure
6
shows the results for Case “HL” with
heavy-tailed covariates
\bm{x}
. The results for other cases and light-tailed
\bm{x}

are similar to those of the
\ell_{1}
penalty and are provided in the supplementary material.

Figure

shows that

with the
\ell_{2}
penalty is
unstable for the Case “HL,” where bias terms are proportional to the leverage
scores of heavy-tailed covariates. In contrast, this instability does not occur
with the
\ell_{1}
penalty, showing its superiority over the
\ell_{2}
penalty in
cases where biases are related to heavy-tailed covariates.
To directly compare the
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
penalties, we present the results
from the two penalties side-by-side in Figure
7
for the Case
“HL” with heavy-tailed covariates
\bm{x}
. For clearer presentation, we only
plot
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}
,
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}
,
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{c(d)}}
, and
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{full}}
. It
is evident that estimators with the
\ell_{1}
penalty are more efficient, except
for
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}
. Note that
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}
does not use external data points with large
outliers with a large probability, whereas
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}
almost always
includes external data points with large outliers. This suggests that the

\ell_{1}
penalty is more effective than the
\ell_{2}
penalty in handling large
outliers in transfer learning. However, we should clarify that the
\ell_{2}

penalty has an advantage over the
\ell_{1}
penalty in terms of computational
simplicity.

6.2
Real Data

We apply our proposed methods to predict airplane hard landings using quick
access recorder (QAR) data. Our dataset includes three types of airplanes:
Airbus A380 (A380), Airbus A320 (A320), and Boeing 737 (B737). Each data point
contains 102 covariates extracted from the QAR records. We consider the maximum
vertical acceleration during landing as a numerical measure of hard landings.
The dataset consists of 1,218 observations for A380; 80,814 observations for
A320; and 68,986 observations for B737. Our goal is to predict the maximum
vertical accelerations during landing for A380 aircrafts using a linear
regression model with other extracted covariates. Accordingly, we treat the A380
data as the target data and merge the A320 and B737 data as the external data.
Since A320 and B737 share similarities with A380 but are still distinct, there
are potential biases in using a model trained on A320 and B737 data to predict
hard landings for A380. We employ the proposed transfer learning methods to
address this.

We repeat our experiments
K=500
times, In each iteration, we randomly split
the target data into training data and test data in each iteration. Two
splitting percentages are considered: 50%-50% and 80%-20%, which correspond
to the training target data size
n_{\mathcal{T}}=609
and
n_{\mathcal{T}}=975
, respectively.

In each iteration, we use marginal t-tests to find important covariates that
affect the maximum vertical acceleration based on the training data by
controlling the false discovery rate (FDR) to be 0.1. Marginal t-tests select
7.332 variables on average for the 50%-50% split and 8.496 variables on
average for the 80%-20% split. The two splitting strategy select the same
seven variables with the highest frequencies. We present them in the following
Table
1
.

Table 1
:
Variables that are selected with most frequency

Code

Definition

Type

Speed

The true parameters are unknown for real data. We evaluate the performances of
the transfer learning estimators by the empirical trimmed prediction error
(eMSPE) which is defined as

\mathrm{eMSPE}(\hat{\bm{\beta}})=\text{Trim}\left(\left\{\frac{1}{\#\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{test}}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{test}}}(y_{i}-\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{k})^{2}:k=1,\ldots,K\right\}\right),

where
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{test}}
represents the index set of the test data.

For combined estimators, we try different proportions of the external data
selected by random subsampling and target-guided.
Specifically, letting
c
be
the proportion of observations selected by the target-guided selection, we consider
c=0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9
. We present the
results of
c=0.1,0.3
in the main paper and leave other results in the
supplementary material to save space.

for an estimator
\hat{\bm{\beta}}
.

From Table
2
, we observe that transfer learning estimators generally
result in positive transfer. When the target training data is small in the
50%-50% split, transfer learning estimators provide more significant
improvements over the estimators that rely solely on target data, compared with
the 80%-20% split. This suggests that transfer learning estimators are
especially advantageous when target data itself is limited and insufficient for
reliable prediction. We also see that although
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}
might perform well when
the sampling rate is low, the combined estimators are more robust and less
likely to result in negative transfer. For example, the combined estimators

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{c(d)}}
and
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{c(e)}}
perform well even when we choose
c

as small as
c=0.1
for the 50%-50% split, while estimators

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{uni}}
and
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}
may result in negative transfer when the
sampling rate is high.

7
Conclusion

This paper addressed the challenges of transfer learning and subsampling in the
presence of data contamination. We focused on scenarios where external data
contain outliers resulting from arbitrary mean shifts, and investigated robust
subsampling strategies to mitigate their influence. Two distinct subsampling
approaches were introduced: one designed to reduce model bias caused by
outliers, and the other to minimize variance.

Our theoretical analysis provided valuable insights into the interplay of
several key factors, including the sizes of the subsamples, the sampling rates,
the magnitude and frequency of outliers, and the tail behavior of the model
error distribution. Importantly, our analysis demonstrated that, under specific
conditions, estimators based on carefully selected subsamples can outperform
those using the full, contaminated data. This highlights the importance of
strategic subsampling in robust data fusion. To further enhance performance, we
proposed methods that combine these bias-reduction and variance-reduction
strategies. Numerical results demonstrate that our approach can effectively
leverage external data to improve the efficiency of target data analysis.

Future research will explore extending these methodologies to nonlinear models,
such as logistic regression models. A key challenge lies in defining appropriate
target-guidance criteria for data selection in the nonlinear context, moving
beyond the residual-based approach used for linear models. Furthermore, we plan
to investigate the application of our methods to high-dimensional datasets,
where the challenges of data contamination and model complexity are amplified.

Appendix A
Supplementary Material

This supplementary material contains the proofs of all theorems and additional
numerical results.

A.1
Mathematical Details of Proposition
1
and Proposition
4

In this section, we prove Proposition
1
and
Proposition
4
simultaneously due to their close connection.

Proof of Proposition
1
and Proposition
4
.

The proof is similar to the proof of Proposition 4.1
in
She and Owen 2011
. To prove the results, we first show that the minimizer
defined in (
3
) satisfies the K-K-T condition of the objective
function (
4
). Then, we will prove that the fixed point of
iterative equation in (
23
) and (
24
) also satisfies the
K-K-T condition. Note that due to the definition of thresholding function

\Theta(t;\lambda)
, we know that the minimizer
(\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{\top},\bm{\gamma}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top})^{\top}

defined in (
3
) should satisfies the following equations:

\displaystyle\bm{V}\hat{\bm{\beta}}=\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}+\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}),\text{ and, }\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}=\Theta(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}};\lambda).

Therefore, by noticing that
\Theta(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}};\lambda)=(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}})-\psi\left(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}};\lambda\right)
,
we have that

\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}+\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\psi\left(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}};\lambda\right)=\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}(\bm{y}_{\Xi}-\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}})=\bm{V}\hat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}
.
Since
\bm{V}=\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}+\bm{V}_{\Xi}^{w}
where

\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}=\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}
and
\bm{V}_{\Xi}^{w}=\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}
, by
rearranging the terms, we
obtain that the minimizer
(\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{\top},\bm{\gamma}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top})^{\top}
defined
in (
3
) satisfies

\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}(\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\hat{\bm{\beta}})+\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\psi\left(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}};\lambda\right)=0,

(S.26)

which is the K-K-T condition of (
4
) by the definition of

\mathcal{H}(t;\lambda)
. Next, we prove that the fix point of iterative equation
in (
23
) and (
24
) also satisfies (
S.26
).
We know that
\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}
is the fixed point of the following equation

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}=\Theta\left\{(\bm{I}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}})\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}+\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}};\lambda\right\},

(S.27)

and
\hat{\bm{\beta}}=\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}+\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}})
. Therefore,
direct calculation gives

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}(\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\hat{\bm{\beta}})+\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\psi\left(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}};\lambda\right)

\displaystyle=\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}})

\displaystyle\quad+\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{V}_{\Xi^{*}}^{w}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{V}_{\Xi^{*}}^{w}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}})

\displaystyle\quad-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\Theta\left\{\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}});\lambda\right\}

\displaystyle=\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}})+\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}=\bm{0},

which complete the proof.
∎

To ease the notation, we denote

\mathcal{L}(\bm{\beta})=n_{\Xi}^{-1}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathcal{H}(y_{e}-\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta};\lambda)
and

\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{D}}(\bm{\beta})=\frac{1}{r}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\delta_{e}\mathcal{H}(y_{e}-\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta};\lambda),\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta})=\frac{1}{r}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\mathcal{H}(y_{e}-\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta};\lambda),

in the rest of the appendix.

A.2
The toy model to illustrate the drawback of OSMAC

We use a simulation on a toy model to illustrate the potential inefficient
performances of existing optimal subsampling probabilities. We generate target
data with size 20 and external data with size 2000. The covariates
\bm{x}
of the
target and external data are 9-dimensional random vectors generated from normal
distribution with variance 4. The covariates are independent. The parameter

\bm{\beta}_{0}
is a 9-dimensional vector with elements equal to 1. For both the target and
the external data, the error terms are generated from normal distribution with
variances equal to 1 and we randomly select 25% data points of the external
data to be contaminated by adding a bias terms drawn from
\Gamma(3,3)
. We
compute the transfer learning estimator based on (
5
). We compare
three
\hat{\bm{\beta}}
, one only uses the target data, one uses data uniformly selected
from the external data and the another uses data randomly selected from
OSMAC. The results are summarized into the following
Table
S.3
. We use empirical MSE as a measure of performances and
the numbers in the brackets are the percentage of selected data points that are
contaminated.

Table S.3
:
Inefficient performance of OSMAC

Subsample size

full (
100n_{\mathcal{T}}
)

0.704 (0.789)

As shown in Table
S.3
. Existing optimal sampling method
performs even worse than uniform sampling and estimator without
transfer learning. The main reason is that OSMAC include too many data points
with biases. For uniform sampling, only 25% of the selected data are
contaminated which is similar to the true contaminated rate. However, there
are about 79% data points selected by OSMAC are contaminated, which causes the
OSMAC performs even worse than the estimator with no transfer learning.

A.3
Mathematical Details of Section
3

In this section, we present the detailed proofs of theoretical analysis in
Section
3
. We begin with some lemmas for the later proofs in
Section
A.3.1
. We present the proof of Theorem
1
in
Section
A.3.2
and We the proof of Theorem
2
in
Section
A.3.3
. In Section, we present the proofs of
Corollary
1
and Proposition
2
.

A.3.1
Lemmas for the proofs

Lemma S.1
.

Under Assumption
1
, for any
n>0
,
\rho>0
and
0<\pi_{i}\leq 1
,

1\leq i\leq n
, assuming
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\pi_{i}=\rho n

w_{i}=(\rho\delta_{i})/\pi_{i}
,

\min_{1\leq i\leq n}\pi_{i}\geq c_{\pi}\rho
, and

\mathbb{V}(\varepsilon_{i})=\sigma^{2}
, where
\delta_{i}=I(u_{i}\leq\pi_{i})
and

u_{i}\sim U[0,1]
, we have that with probability at least

1-e^{-\varsigma}-C/\sqrt{\rho n}
,
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}/\rho n\geq 1/8

and
(\rho n)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{d+\varsigma}
, where
C
is a constant that does not depend
on
n
.

Proof.

Note that for any
\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, where
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
represents
thee unit sphere in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have that due to Chebyshev’s
inequality, for any
c>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{\rho n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}\bm{u}-1\right|>c\right)\leq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{V}(w_{i}\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}\bm{u})}{(\rho n)^{2}c^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{V}(w_{i}\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}\bm{u}|\bm{x}_{i})\right\}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{V}\left\{\mathbb{E}(w_{i}\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}\bm{u}|\bm{x}_{i})\right\}}{(\rho n)^{2}c^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u})^{2}\right\}}{\rho c_{\pi}nc^{2}}+\frac{\mathbb{V}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u})}{\rho nc^{2}}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2}^{4})}{c^{2}(c_{\pi}\wedge 1)\rho n}.

Thus, taking
c=7/8
, we have that with probability at least
1-C_{1}/\rho n
,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}/\rho n\geq 1/8
, where
C_{1}
does not depend on

n
. Denoting

\bm{W}_{i}=\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\varepsilon_{i}^{2}}{\pi_{i}}\right)\right\}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{w_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}}{\rho},

we have that
\mathbb{E}(\bm{W}_{i})=\bm{0}
and

\mathbb{V}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{W}_{i})=\bm{I}_{d}
. Applying Theorem 1.1
in
Raič 2019
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{W}_{i}\right\|_{2}>\sqrt{d+8\varsigma}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbb{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I}_{d})\|_{2}>\sqrt{d+8\varsigma})+\frac{C_{2}d^{\frac{1}{4}}(\sqrt{\rho})^{3}}{(\sqrt{n})^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathbb{E}(\|\delta_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2}^{3})}{\pi_{i}^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\chi_{d}^{2}>d+\varsigma\right)+\frac{C_{2}\mathbb{E}\{|\varepsilon|^{3}(\sqrt{\rho})^{3}\}\mathbb{E}\left\{\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2}^{3}\right\}}{\rho^{2}c_{\pi}^{2}\sqrt{n}}\leq e^{-\varsigma}+\frac{C_{3}}{\sqrt{\rho n}}.

Therefore, we know that with probability at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}-C_{3}/\sqrt{\rho n}
,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}/\rho n\geq 1/8
and

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\varepsilon_{i}^{2}}{\pi_{i}}\right)\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{W}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{c_{\pi}}}\left\|\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\right)\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{W}_{i}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{c_{\pi}}}\left\|\sqrt{n}\sigma\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{W}_{i}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\rho n}\sigma\sqrt{d+\varsigma}.

∎

Lemma S.2
.

Under Assumption
1
, assuming that
\min_{e\in\Xi}\pi_{e}\geq\rho c_{\pi}
,
for any fixed
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{n_{\Xi}}
and
0<\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\leq 1
, there exists a constant
C
does not depend on
n_{\Xi}
, such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho n_{\Xi}}\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\left\{\psi_{1}(\gamma_{e}+\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)-\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\right\}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{C\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}}{\lambda^{\alpha_{1}}}+\frac{C\lambda(d+\varsigma)}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}+C\lambda^{\alpha_{2}}s_{\gamma,\alpha_{2}},

with probability at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}
.

Proof.

By defining
\bm{\xi}^{*}=n_{\Xi}^{-1}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\left\{\xi_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}-\mathbb{E}(\xi_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})\right\}
, where

\xi_{e}=\pi_{e}^{-1}\delta_{e}\{\psi_{1}(\gamma_{e}+\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)-\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\}
,
our
outline of this proof is to first bound
\bm{\xi}^{*}
using Bernstein’s
inequality and then bound
n_{\Xi}^{-1}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})
. We start with the
bound of
\bm{\xi}^{*}
. For function
\psi_{1}(x;\lambda)
, it is easy to verify
that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|\psi_{1}(x+y;\lambda)-\psi_{1}(x;\lambda)|=|y|I(|y|\leq 2\lambda)+2\lambda I(|y|>2\lambda).

(S.28)

According to the proof of the proof of Lemma 6 in
Sun et al. 2020
, we
know that there exits a
1/2
-net

\mathcal{N}_{1/2}
of the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with

\#\mathcal{N}_{1/2}\leq 5^{d}
such that
\|\bm{\xi}^{*}\|_{2}\leq 2\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathcal{N}_{1/2}}|\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\xi}^{*}|
. Under
Assumption
1
, since
\tilde{\bm{x}}
is sub-gaussian, for every

\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have that
\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{k}\leq C_{0}^{k}k\Gamma(k/2)
for all
k\geq 1
, where
\Gamma(\cdot)
represents the Gamma
function. We also have that for
0<\alpha_{1}\leq 1
,

k\geq 2
, and any fixed
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{n_{\Xi}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\left\{\psi_{1}(\gamma_{e}+\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)-\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\right\}\right|^{k}\leq\frac{1}{\pi_{e}^{k-1}}\left\{|\gamma_{e}|^{k}I(|\gamma_{e}|\leq 2\lambda)+2^{k}\lambda^{k}I(|\gamma_{e}|>2\lambda)\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi_{e}^{k-1}}\left\{|\gamma_{e}|^{k-1-\alpha_{1}}|\gamma_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}I(|\gamma_{e}|\leq 2\lambda)+(2\lambda)^{k-1-\alpha_{1}}(2\lambda)^{1+\alpha_{1}}I(|\gamma_{e}|>2\lambda)\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi_{e}^{k-1}}(2\lambda)^{k-1-\alpha_{1}}|\gamma_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}.

The inequality holds for both cases that
\gamma_{e}=0
and
\gamma_{e}\neq 0
. Therefore, we have that since

\sum_{e\in\Xi}\pi_{e}^{-1}|\gamma_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}=\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}\pi_{e}^{-1}|\gamma_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}=n_{\Xi}\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\xi_{e}\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|^{k}\right\}\leq C_{0}^{k}k\Gamma\left(\frac{k}{2}\right)(2\lambda)^{k-1-\alpha_{1}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{|\gamma_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}}{\pi_{e}^{k-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{C_{0}}{\rho c_{\pi}}\right)^{k-2}k\Gamma\left(\frac{k}{2}\right)(2\lambda)^{k-2}2C_{0}^{2}(2\lambda)^{1-\alpha_{1}}n_{\Xi}\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}\leq\frac{k!}{2}\left(\frac{C_{0}\lambda}{\rho c_{\pi}}\right)^{k-2}2C_{0}^{2}(2\lambda)^{1-\alpha_{1}}n_{\Xi}\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}},

for any
k\geq 2
. A special case is when
k=2
, we have that

\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\xi_{e}\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|^{2}\right\}\leq 2C_{0}^{2}(2\lambda)^{1-\alpha_{1}}n_{\Xi}\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}
. Now,
due to Bernstein’s inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{|\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\xi}^{*}|\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{(2\lambda)^{1-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}\varsigma}{n_{\Xi}}}+\frac{C_{0}\lambda\varsigma}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}\right\}\leq 2e^{-\varsigma},

for any
\varsigma>0
. Taking union over
\bm{u}\in\mathcal{N}_{1/2}
, we obtain that
with probability at least
1-2\times 5^{d}e^{-\varsigma}
,

\|\bm{\xi}^{*}\|_{2}\leq 8C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\lambda^{1-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}\varsigma}{n_{\Xi}}}+\frac{2C_{0}\lambda\varsigma}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}\leq 4C_{0}\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\rho\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}+\frac{6C_{0}\lambda\varsigma}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}.

The last inequality is because
2\sqrt{(\lambda\varsigma n_{\Xi}^{-1})(\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})}\leq\lambda\varsigma\rho^{-1}n_{\Xi}^{-1}+\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\rho\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}

and
r=\sum_{e\in\Xi}\pi_{e}\geq\rho n_{\Xi}c_{\pi}=rc_{\pi}
. Next, we bound

n_{\Xi}^{-1}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})
. Since

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\xi_{e}|\leq|\gamma_{e}|I(|\gamma_{e}|\leq 2\lambda)+2\lambda I(|\gamma_{e}|>2\lambda)\leq(2\lambda)^{\alpha_{2}}|\gamma_{e}|^{1-\alpha_{2}}+\frac{|\gamma_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}}{(2\lambda)^{\alpha_{1}}\pi_{e}},

and
\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|\leq 2C_{0}
, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left(|\xi_{e}\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|\right)\leq 2C_{0}\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}+4C_{0}\lambda^{\alpha_{2}}s_{\gamma,\alpha_{2}}.

Now, by taking
\varsigma=2(d+\varsigma)
and
C=6C_{0}
, we
have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\left\{\psi_{1}(\gamma_{e}+\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)-\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\right\}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{C\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}}{\lambda^{\alpha_{1}}}+\frac{C\lambda\varsigma}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}+C\lambda^{\alpha_{2}}s_{\gamma,\alpha_{2}}.

with probability at least
1-2\times 5^{d}e^{-2d-2\varsigma}\geq 1-2e^{-2\varsigma}\geq 1-e^{-\varsigma}
.
∎

Lemma S.3
.

Under Assumption
1
, if
\lambda,R
satisfy
\lambda\gtrsim(\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{1}}}\vee(\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{1}}}\vee(8C_{1}^{2}R)

and
n_{\Xi}\gtrsim\left(\lambda/R\right)^{2}(d+\varsigma)
,
we will have that with probability at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}
,

\left\{\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta})-\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}^{\top}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})\geq 8^{-1}\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}
,
uniformly over

\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(R)=\left\{\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}\leq R\right\}
.

Proof.

We first state the outline of this proof. We consider a lower bound of

\mathcal{T}(\bm{\beta}):=\left\{\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta})-\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}^{\top}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})

We decompose the lower bound into
an expectation part and a deviation part and then bound the two parts
separately. First, one of the lower bound of
\mathcal{T}(\bm{\beta})
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(\bm{\beta})\geq\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\left\{\psi_{1}(y_{e}-\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{0};\lambda)-\psi_{1}(y_{e}-\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta};\lambda)\right\}\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})I(\mathcal{E}_{e}),

where
\mathcal{E}_{e}=\left\{|\gamma_{e}+\varepsilon_{e}|\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\right\}\cap\left\{|\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})|\leq\lambda\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}/2R\right\}
.
It is easy to verify that under event
\mathcal{E}_{e}
, we have

|y_{e}-\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{0}|\leq\lambda
and
|y_{e}-\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta}|\leq\lambda
. Therefore,

\mathcal{T}(\bm{\beta})\geq n_{\Xi}^{-1}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\pi_{e}^{-1}\delta_{e}\left\{\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}^{2}I(\mathcal{E}_{e})
.
Denoting
\phi_{A}(x)=x^{2}I(|x|\leq A/2)+(x-A)^{2}I(A/2\leq x\leq A)+(x+A)^{2}I(-A\leq x\leq-A/2)

It is easy to verify that
x^{2}I\left(|x|\leq 0.5A\right)\leq\phi_{A}(x)\leq x^{2}I(|x|\leq A)
and
0\leq\phi_{A}(x)\leq 0.25A^{2}
.
Therefore, we have a lower bound of
\mathcal{T}(\bm{\beta})
:

\mathcal{T}(\bm{\beta})\geq g(\bm{\beta}):=\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}g_{e}(\bm{\beta})=\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\phi_{\frac{\lambda\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}}{2R}}\left\{\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e}|\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\right).

Next, we decompose the lower bound of
\mathcal{T}(\bm{\beta})
into two parts, an
expectation part and a deviation part. Letting

\Delta(r)=\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(r)}|g(\bm{\beta})-\mathbb{E}\left\{g(\bm{\beta})\right\}|/\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}
as the deviation part, we have that

\frac{\mathcal{T}(\bm{\beta})}{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}}\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left\{g(\bm{\beta})\right\}}{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}}-\Delta(r).

We thus bound the two parts separately. We first bound

\mathbb{E}\left\{g(\bm{\beta})\right\}
. Note that, denoting
\bm{\eta}=\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}
, we
have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{g(\bm{\beta})\right\}=\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\phi_{\frac{\lambda\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}}{2R}}\left\{\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e}|\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi-\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_{\frac{\lambda\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}}{2R}}(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta})I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}|\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)\right]+\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_{\frac{\lambda\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}}{2R}}(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta})I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e}|\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_{\frac{\lambda\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}}{2R}}(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta})\right]-\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi-\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_{\frac{\lambda\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}}{2R}}(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta})I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}|>\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_{\frac{\lambda\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}}{2R}}(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta})I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e}|>\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta})^{2}-\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta})^{2}I\left(|\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta}|>\frac{\lambda\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}}{4R}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi-\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta}\right)^{2}I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}|>\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)\right\}-\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta}\right)^{2}I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e}|>\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta})^{2}-\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta})^{2}I\left(|\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta}|>\frac{\lambda\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}}{4R}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi-\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta}\right)^{2}I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}|>\frac{\lambda}{4}\right)\right\}-\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta}\right)^{2}I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}|>\frac{\lambda}{4}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta}\right)^{2}I\left(|\gamma_{e}|>\frac{\lambda}{4}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\bm{\eta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\eta}-\left(\frac{4R}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}(\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{\eta})^{4}}{\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}}-\left(\frac{4}{\lambda}\right)^{1+\alpha_{1}}\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}})\bm{\eta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\eta}-\left(\frac{4}{\lambda}\right)^{1+\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}\bm{\eta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\eta}

\displaystyle\geq\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}\left\{1-\left(\frac{4}{\lambda}\right)^{1+\alpha_{1}}\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}})-\left(\frac{4}{\lambda}\right)^{1+\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}-\left(\frac{4C_{1}^{2}R}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\right\}\geq\frac{1}{4}\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}.

as long as
\lambda\gtrsim(\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{1}}}\vee(\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{1}}}\vee(8C_{1}^{2}R)
. Next,
we consider a bound of the deviation part
\Delta(R)
. Since
0\leq I(|\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e}|\leq\lambda/2)\leq 1
and
\phi_{A}(x)\leq A^{2}/4
, we know that
0\leq g_{e}(\bm{\beta})\leq(4R)^{-2}(\rho c_{\pi})^{-1}\lambda^{2}\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}

Then, by Theorem 7.3 in
Bousquet 2003
, we have that for any

\varsigma>0
,

\displaystyle\Delta(R)\leq\mathbb{E}\{\Delta(R)\}+\mathbb{E}\{\Delta(R)\}^{1/2}\frac{\lambda}{2R}\sqrt{\frac{\varsigma}{\rho c_{\pi}n_{\Xi}}}+\sigma_{n_{\Xi}}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{2\varsigma}{n_{\Xi}}}+\left(\frac{\lambda}{4R}\right)^{2}\frac{\varsigma}{3\rho c_{\pi}n_{\Xi}},

with probability at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}
with

\displaystyle\sigma_{n_{\Xi}}^{2}=\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(R)}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left\{g_{e}^{2}(\bm{\beta})\right\}}{\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{4}}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{\eta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{4}\right\}}{\rho^{2}c_{\pi}^{2}\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{4}}\leq\rho^{-2}c_{\pi}^{-2}C_{1}^{4}.

To bound
\mathbb{E}\left\{\Delta(R)\right\}
,
using symmetrization inequality and the connection between Gaussian complexity
and Rademacher complexity, we have
\mathbb{E}\left\{\Delta(R)\right\}\leq\sqrt{2\pi}\rho^{-1}c_{\pi}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(R)}|\mathbb{G}_{\bm{\beta}}|\right\}
,
where

\mathbb{G}_{\bm{\beta}}=\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{G_{e}}{\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}}\phi_{\frac{\lambda\|\bm{\eta}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}}{2R}}(\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\eta})I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e}|\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\right),

and
G_{e}
are i.i.d. standard normal random variables that are independent of

\{\varepsilon_{e},\bm{x}_{e}\}_{b=1}^{n_{\Xi}}
. Thus, using exactly the same argument in the
proof of Lemma 4 in
Sun et al. 2020
, we have that

\mathbb{E}\{\Delta(R)\}\leq\sqrt{2\pi}\rho^{-1}c_{\pi}^{-1}\left\{2\lambda R^{-1}d^{\frac{1}{2}}n_{\Xi}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\lambda(4R)^{-1}n_{\Xi}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\}
.
Therefore, as long as we take
n_{\Xi}\gtrsim(\lambda/R)^{2}(d+\varsigma)
, we have that

\Delta(R)\leq 1/8
and thus, with probability at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}
,

\frac{\mathcal{T}(\bm{\beta})}{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}}\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left\{g(\bm{\beta})\right\}}{\|\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}^{2}}-\Delta(r)\geq\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{8}=\frac{1}{8}.

∎

Lemma S.4
.

Under Assumption
1
, assuming that
\min_{e\in\Xi}\pi_{e}\geq c_{\pi}\rho
,
for any
0<\alpha_{1}\leq 1
,
there exists a constant
C
does not depend on
n_{\Xi}
, such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\left\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\bm{x}_{e}\right\|_{2}\leq C\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}+\frac{C\lambda(d+\varsigma)}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}

with probability at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}
.

Proof.

By defining
\bm{\xi}^{*}=n_{\Xi}^{-1}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\left\{\xi_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i}-\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i})\right\}
, where

\xi_{e}=\pi_{e}^{-1}\delta_{e}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)
.
Similar to the proof of Lemma
S.2
, there exits a
1/2
-net

\mathcal{N}_{1/2}
of the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with

\#\mathcal{N}_{1/2}\leq 5^{d}
such that
\|\bm{\xi}^{*}\|_{2}\leq 2\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathcal{N}_{1/2}}|\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\xi}^{*}|
. Therefore, for

0<\alpha_{1}\leq 1
,
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\right|^{k}=\frac{1}{\pi_{e}^{k-1}}\mathbb{E}\left|\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\right|^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\pi_{e}^{k-1}}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\varepsilon_{e}|^{k}I(|\varepsilon_{e}|\leq\lambda)+\lambda^{k}I(|\varepsilon_{e}|>\lambda)\right\}\leq\frac{1}{\pi_{e}^{k-1}}\lambda^{k-1-\alpha_{1}}|\varepsilon_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}.

The inequality holds for both cases that
\gamma_{e}=0
and
\gamma_{e}\neq 0
. Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\xi_{e}\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|^{k}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}^{k}k\Gamma\left(\frac{k}{2}\right)\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}|\bm{x}_{i})}{\pi_{e}^{k-1}}\lambda^{k-1-\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k!}{2}\left(\frac{C_{0}\lambda}{\rho c_{\pi}}\right)^{k-2}2C_{0}^{2}\lambda^{1-\alpha_{1}}c_{\pi}^{-1}n_{\Xi}\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}},

for any
k\geq 2
. A special case is when
k=2
, we have that

\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\xi_{e}\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|^{2}\right\}\leq 2C_{0}^{2}\lambda^{1-\alpha_{1}}c_{\pi}^{-1}n_{\Xi}\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}
. Now due to
Bernstein’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{|\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\xi}^{*}|\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\lambda^{1-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}\varsigma}{c_{\pi}n_{\Xi}}}+\frac{C_{0}\lambda\varsigma}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}\right\}\leq 2e^{-\varsigma},

for any
\varsigma>0
. Taking union over
\bm{u}\in\mathcal{N}_{1/2}
, we obtain that
with probability at least
1-2\times 5^{d}e^{-\varsigma}
,

\|\bm{\xi}^{*}\|_{2}\leq 4C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\lambda^{1-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}\varsigma}{c_{\pi}n_{\Xi}}}+\frac{2C_{0}\lambda\varsigma}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}.\leq 2C_{0}\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\rho\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}+\frac{4C_{0}\lambda\varsigma}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}.

The last inequality is because
2\sqrt{(\lambda\varsigma n_{\Xi}^{-1}c_{\pi}^{-1})(\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\rho\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})}\leq\lambda\varsigma\rho^{-1}n_{\Xi}^{-1}c_{\pi}^{-1}+\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\rho\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}
. For

n_{\Xi}^{-1}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})
, due to (44) in
Sun et al. 2020
, we have
that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\left|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left(\xi_{e}\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\left|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{0}\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}|\bm{x}_{i})

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{0}\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{|\varepsilon_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}}{\pi_{e}}\Big|\bm{x}_{i}\right)\leq 2C_{0}\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}.

Therefore, by taking
\varsigma=2(d+\varsigma)
and
C=4C_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\left\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\bm{x}_{e}\right\|_{2}\leq C\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}+\frac{C\lambda(d+\varsigma)}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}},

with probability at least
1-2\times 5^{d}e^{-2d-2\varsigma}\geq 1-2e^{-2\varsigma}\geq 1-e^{-\varsigma}
.
∎

A.3.2
Proof of Theorem
1

Proof of Theorem
1
.

For any prespecified
R>0
, we build an estimator

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}=\bm{\beta}_{0}+\eta(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})
, such that

\|\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|\leq R
. To be more specific, we take
\eta=1
if

\|\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{2}\leq R
; otherwise, we choose some
\eta\in(0,1)
so that

\|\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{2}=R
. Due to Lemma 2 in
Sun et al. 2020
, we know that

\displaystyle\rho n_{\Xi}\left\{\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta})-\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\eta\rho n_{\Xi}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}})^{\top}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})-\eta\rho n_{\Xi}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta}_{0})(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})

\displaystyle=-(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\psi_{1}\left(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}};\lambda\right)+(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\psi_{1}\left(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{\beta}_{0};\lambda\right).

We have that the K-K-T condition in (
S.26
) implies

\displaystyle\rho n_{\Xi}\left\{\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta})-\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\eta(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}(\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}})+\eta(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\psi_{1}\left(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{\beta}_{0};\lambda\right)

\displaystyle=\eta(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{\beta}_{0}+\bm{\varepsilon}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}})+\eta(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\psi_{1}\left(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{\beta}_{0};\lambda\right)

\displaystyle=\eta(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{\varepsilon}_{\mathcal{T}}-(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})+\eta(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e})\bm{x}_{e}.

Rearranging the terms, we obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho n_{\Xi}\left\{\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta})-\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})+(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})\\
&\leq\eta(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{\varepsilon}_{\mathcal{T}}+\eta(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e})\bm{x}_{e}.\end{split}

(S.29)

Applying Lemma
S.3
and taking
R=\lambda/(8C_{1}^{2})
, there
exists a constant
C_{1}
that does not depend on sample sizes such that as long
as
\lambda\geq C_{1}\{(\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{1}}}\vee(\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{1}}}\}
and
n_{\Xi}\geq C_{1}(d+\varsigma)
, we
have that with probability at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}
,

\rho n_{\Xi}\left\{\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta})-\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})\geq\frac{1}{8}\rho n_{\Xi}\|\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}.

(S.30)

Taking
n=n_{\mathcal{T}}
,
\rho=1
and
\pi_{t}=n_{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}
for
t\in\mathcal{T}
,we have that with
probability at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}-1/\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}
,

(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})\geq(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})\geq\frac{1}{8}n_{\mathcal{T}}\|\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2},

(S.31)

and
I_{\mathcal{T}}\leq C_{2}\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}\sqrt{(d+\varsigma)n_{\mathcal{T}}}
, where
C_{2}
does not depend
on sample sizes. On the other hand, from Lemma
S.4
, we know that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho n_{\Xi}}\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{3}\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}+\frac{C_{3}\lambda(d+\varsigma)}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}},

where
C_{3}
does not depend on
n_{\Xi}
with probability at least

1-e^{-\varsigma}
. From Lemma
S.2
, we know that with probability
at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho n_{\Xi}}\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\left\{\psi_{1}(\gamma_{e}+\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)-\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\right\}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{4}\lambda^{-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}+\frac{C_{4}\lambda(d+\varsigma)}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}+C_{4}\lambda^{\alpha_{2}}s_{\gamma,\alpha_{2}},

where
C_{4}
does not depend on
n_{\Xi}
. Now, since
\lambda=\left\{(\rho c_{\pi}n_{\Xi})/(d+\varsigma)\right\}^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{1}}}
,
we have that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\rho n_{\Xi}}\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{3}(\sigma_{\varepsilon,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}\vee 1)\left(\frac{d+\varsigma}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{1+\alpha_{1}}},\end{split}

(S.32)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\rho n_{\Xi}}\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\left\{\psi_{1}(\gamma_{e}+\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)-\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\right\}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right\|_{2}\\
&\leq C_{4}(\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}\vee 1)\left(\frac{d+\varsigma}{c_{\pi}\rho n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{1+\alpha_{1}}}+C_{4}\lambda^{\alpha_{2}}s_{\gamma,\alpha_{2}}.\end{split}

(S.33)

Therefore, combining (
S.29
), (
S.30
),
(
S.31
), (
S.32
), (
S.33
), we have that, taking

R=\lambda/8C_{1}^{2}
,

\|\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}\lesssim\frac{n_{\mathcal{T}}}{N_{f}}\sqrt{\frac{d+\varsigma}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}+\frac{r}{N_{f}}\left(\frac{d+\varsigma}{r}\right)^{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{1+\alpha_{1}}}+\frac{r}{N_{f}}\lambda^{\alpha_{2}}s_{\gamma,\alpha_{2}}:=R_{0,1}+R_{0,2}+R_{0,3}:=R_{0}.

Note that as long as
R_{0}<R
, we have that
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}
lies in the interior
of the ball with radius
R
and by the construction of
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}
, we have
that
\eta=1
in this case, which implies

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\eta}=\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}
. Therefore, we prove that

R_{0,1},R_{0,2},R_{0,3}<R/3
, respectively. Note that

\frac{\sqrt{d+\varsigma}}{2\sqrt{r}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{d+\varsigma}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}+\frac{r}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}{N_{f}}\sqrt{d+\varsigma},

and therefore as long as
r\gtrsim(d+\varsigma)
, we have that

R_{1,0}<R/3
. Since
R_{0,2}\sim(r/N_{f})\lambda(d+\varsigma)/r
, it is also
easy to know that
R_{0,2}<R/3
as long as
(n_{\mathcal{T}}+r)\gtrsim(d+\varsigma)
. The fact
that
R_{0,3}<R/3
is due to the fact that

N_{f}r^{\frac{-\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}}{1+\alpha_{1}}}\gtrsim s_{\gamma,\alpha_{2}}(d+\varsigma)^{\frac{1-\alpha_{2}}{1+\alpha_{1}}}
.
Thus, we
complete the proof of the bound.

∎

A.3.3
Proof of Theorem
2

Proof of Theorem
2
.

From Proposition
1
, we know that when
P(\cdot;\lambda)
is

\ell_{2}
penalty, the corresponding
\mathcal{H}(t;\lambda)
is

\mathcal{H}(t;\lambda)=\frac{\lambda t^{2}}{1+\lambda}.

Thus, we consider a more general form of weighted transfer learning estimator
which minimizes

\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}(y_{t}-\bm{x}_{t}^{\top}\bm{\beta})^{2}+\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\mathcal{H}(y_{e}-\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta};\lambda)=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}(y_{t}-\bm{x}_{t}^{\top}\bm{\beta})^{2}+\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\frac{\lambda(y_{e}-\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta})^{2}}{1+\lambda}.

Directly solve the optimization problem, we have that

\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}=\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\bm{x}_{t}\bm{x}_{t}^{\top}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\bm{x}_{e}\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\bm{x}_{t}y_{t}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\bm{x}_{e}y_{e}\right).

Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{\beta}_{0}=\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}y_{t}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}y_{e}\right)-\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{\beta}_{0}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\varepsilon_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\varepsilon_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\gamma_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right).

Letting

\rho^{2}n_{\Xi}\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}\mathbb{V}(\tilde{\bm{x}})\leq\bm{M}=\mathbb{E}\left\{\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho^{2}|\gamma_{e}|^{2}}{\pi_{e}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}^{\top}\right\}-\rho^{2}\sum_{e\in\Xi}|\gamma_{e}|^{2}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{\top}\leq\rho^{2}n_{\Xi}\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}),

and
\bm{W}_{e}=\bm{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\{w_{e}\gamma_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}-\rho\gamma_{e}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})\}
.
Due to Berry-Essen theorem (see Theorem 1.1 in
Raič 2019
), we
have that for every
\varsigma\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\bm{W}_{e}\right\|_{2}>\sqrt{\varsigma}\right)-\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{Z}\|_{2}>\sqrt{\varsigma})\leq\frac{Cd^{\frac{1}{4}}}{(\sqrt{n_{\Xi}})^{3}\rho^{3}(\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{\|w_{e}\gamma_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}-\rho\gamma_{e}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})\|_{2}^{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sum_{e\in\Xi}|\gamma_{e}|^{3}\mathbb{E}(\|w_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\|_{2}^{3})}{(\sqrt{n_{\Xi}})^{3}\rho^{3}(\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{\sum_{e\in\Xi}|\gamma_{e}|^{3}\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2}^{3})}{(\sqrt{n_{\Xi}})^{3}(\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2}^{3})}{(\sqrt{n_{\Xi}})^{3}c_{\pi}\rho(\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{|\gamma_{e}|^{3}}{\pi_{e}}+\frac{\sum_{e\in\Xi}|\gamma_{e}|^{3}\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2}^{3})}{(\sqrt{n_{\Xi}})^{3}(\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2}^{3})}{c_{\pi}\rho(\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\rho n_{\Xi}}}\sigma_{\gamma,2}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2}^{3})}{(\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{n_{\Xi}}}\frac{\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}|\gamma_{e}|^{3}}{n_{\Xi}}\lesssim\frac{\sigma_{\gamma,2}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}}{\sqrt{\rho n_{\Xi}}},

where
\bm{Z}\sim\mathbb{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I}_{d})
. Therefore, we have that with probability at
least
1-e^{-d-\varsigma}-(C_{2}\sigma_{\gamma,2}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})/\sqrt{\rho n_{\Xi}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\gamma_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2})\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}|\gamma_{e}|+d^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\rho n_{\Xi}}\sqrt{\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}}\sqrt{\varsigma}

\displaystyle=\rho n_{\Xi}\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2})s_{\gamma,0}+\sqrt{\rho n_{\Xi}}d^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}}\sqrt{\varsigma}

Applying Lemma
S.1
, by taking
n=n_{\mathcal{T}}
,
\rho=1
and

\pi_{i}=n_{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}
, we have that
\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top}\geq 8^{-1}n_{\mathcal{T}}
and

\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\varepsilon_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}\sqrt{d+\varsigma}
with
probability at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}-C_{3}/\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}
. From
Lemma
S.1
, we can also know that
\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}^{\top}\geq 8^{-1}\rho n_{\Xi}
and
\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\varepsilon_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\rho n_{\Xi}}\sqrt{\sigma_{\varepsilon,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}}\sqrt{d+\varsigma}
with probability at least

1-e^{-\varsigma}-C_{4}/\sqrt{\rho n_{\Xi}}
. Therefore, we have that

\bm{V}_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{N_{f\lambda}}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}^{\top}\right)\geq\frac{1}{8},

where
N_{f\lambda}=n_{\mathcal{T}}+\{\lambda/(1+\lambda)\}\rho n_{\Xi}
, and

\|\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0}\|_{\bm{\Sigma},2}\lesssim\frac{n_{\mathcal{T}}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}+c_{\lambda}r}I_{\mathcal{T}}+\frac{c_{\lambda}r}{n_{\mathcal{T}}+c_{\lambda}r}I_{\Xi}+\frac{c_{\lambda}r}{n_{\mathcal{T}}+c_{\lambda}r}I_{\gamma},

(S.34)

where
I_{\mathcal{T}}=\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}\sqrt{d}\sqrt{\varsigma/n_{\mathcal{T}}}
,

I_{\Xi}=\sqrt{\sigma_{\varepsilon,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}}\sqrt{d}\sqrt{\varsigma/r}

and

I_{\gamma}=s_{\gamma,0}+\sqrt{\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}}\sqrt{d}\sqrt{\varsigma/r}
,
with probability at least

1-Ce^{-\varsigma}-C/\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}-(C\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}})/\sqrt{r}
. Thus,
under the condition that
\sqrt{r}e^{-\varsigma}\gtrsim\sigma_{\gamma,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}
, the inequality in (
S.34
) holds
with probability at least
1-Ce^{-\varsigma}-C/\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}
.

∎

A.3.4
Proofs of Corollary
1
and
Proposition
2

Proof of Corollary
1
(I)
.

To prove Corollary
1
(I), we first define a random
process

\displaystyle\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{N_{f}}\Big\{-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}(\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{\beta})+\rho n_{\Xi}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle\quad+\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}(\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{\beta}_{0})-\rho n_{\Xi}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta}_{0})\Big\}-\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0}).

where
N_{f}=n_{\mathcal{T}}+\rho n_{\Xi}
. We start with a decomposition,

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})-\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{N_{f}}\left\{\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}(\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{\beta}_{0})+\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\bm{x}_{e}\right\}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}}-\bm{\beta}_{0})-\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{N_{f}}\Big\{\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}(\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{\beta}_{0})+\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e};\lambda)\bm{x}_{e}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e};\lambda)\bm{x}_{e}+\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\bm{x}_{e}\Big\}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{B}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}})\|_{2}+\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\left\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\{\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e};\lambda)-\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\}\bm{x}_{e}\right\|_{2},

where the last inequality is because

-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}(\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}-\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}})+\rho n_{\Xi}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{rs}})=\bm{0}
, which coincides the K-K-T
condition in (
S.26
). Note that we have already obtained that

\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\left\{\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e};\lambda)-\psi_{1}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\right\}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right\|\leq\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I^{\pi}_{\gamma}.

(S.35)

with probability at least
1-Ce^{-\varsigma}-1/\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}
. Thus, we only need to bound

\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(R_{0})}\|\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})\|_{2}
. Our approach to bound the
supremum of the empirical process

\{\bm{B}(\bm{\beta}):\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(R_{0})\}
is to bound

\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})-\mathbb{E}\{\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})\}
and
\mathbb{E}\{\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})\}
separately. We first
compute
\mathbb{E}\{\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})\}
. Due to the mean value theorem,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})\}

\displaystyle=\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{N_{f}}\left\{n_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})+\rho n_{\Xi}\mathbb{E}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta})-\rho n_{\Xi}\mathbb{E}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}-\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}\left[\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\nabla^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\tilde{\bm{\beta}})\right\}\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{I}_{d}\right]\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})

\displaystyle\leq R\times\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(R)}\left\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\nabla^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\tilde{\bm{\beta}})\right\}\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{I}_{d}\right\|_{2}

Therefore, denoting
\bm{\delta}=\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})
, we have that or

\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(R)
and
\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\bm{u}^{\top}\left[\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\nabla^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta})\right\}\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{I}_{d}\right]\bm{u}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\bm{u}^{\top}\left[\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}I(|y_{e}-\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta}|\leq\lambda)\bm{x}_{e}\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\right\}\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{I}_{d}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{I(|y_{e}-\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\beta}|>\lambda)(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{I(|\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e}+\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}(\bm{\beta}_{0}-\bm{\beta})|>\lambda)(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi-\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left\{I(|\varepsilon_{e}-\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\delta}|>\lambda)(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{2}\right\}+\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left\{I(|\varepsilon_{e}+\gamma_{e}-\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\delta}|>\lambda)(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\Xi-\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left\{I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}-\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\delta}|>\lambda\right)(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left\{I\left(|\varepsilon_{e}-\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\delta}|>\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{2}\right\}+\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{E}\left\{I\left(|\gamma_{e}|>\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16}{n_{\Xi}\lambda^{2}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon_{e}|^{2})+\frac{16}{n_{\Xi}\lambda^{2}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{\delta}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{2}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{2}\right\}+\frac{2^{1+\alpha_{1}}}{n_{\Xi}\lambda^{1+\alpha_{1}}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}|\gamma_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16}{n_{\Xi}\lambda^{2}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon_{e}|^{2})+\frac{16}{n_{\Xi}\lambda^{2}}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{\delta}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{2}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})^{2}\right\}+\frac{2^{1+\alpha_{1}}}{n_{\Xi}\lambda^{1+\alpha_{1}}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{O}}|\gamma_{e}|^{1+\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq 16\lambda^{-2}\sigma_{\varepsilon,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}+2^{1+\alpha_{1}}\lambda^{-1-\alpha_{1}}\sigma_{\gamma,\alpha_{1}}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}+16C_{1}^{4}\lambda^{-2}R^{2}\leq 16\lambda^{-2}\sigma_{\varepsilon,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}+4I_{\gamma}^{\pi}+16C_{1}^{4}\lambda^{-2}R^{2}.

From the proof of Theorem
1
and

\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}+\sqrt{\rho n_{\Xi}}\leq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}+\rho n_{\Xi}}=\sqrt{2}\sqrt{N_{f}}
,
we know that, there exits a constant
C
, such that

\displaystyle R=C\sqrt{d+\varsigma}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}{N_{f}}+\frac{\sqrt{\rho n_{\Xi}}}{N_{f}}\right)+\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I_{\gamma}\leq\frac{C\sqrt{2(d+\varsigma)}}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}+\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I_{\gamma},

which implies that
R^{3}\leq 32\sqrt{2}C^{3}(d+\varsigma)^{\frac{3}{2}}N_{f}^{-\frac{3}{2}}+8(\rho n_{\Xi})^{3}N_{f}^{-3}(I_{\gamma}^{\pi})^{3}
. Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(R)}\|\mathbb{E}\left\{\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})\right\}\|_{2}\leq\frac{16\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}\lambda^{-2}\sigma_{\varepsilon,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}R+\frac{4\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I_{\gamma}R+\frac{16C_{1}^{4}\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}\lambda^{-2}R^{3}\\
&\leq 16\sqrt{2}C\sigma_{\varepsilon,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}\left(\frac{d+\varsigma}{N_{f}}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}+\frac{16\sqrt{2}C\sigma_{\varepsilon,1}^{{\textrm{\tiny W}}}(d+\varsigma)}{N_{f}}\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I_{\gamma}+4\sqrt{2}C\sqrt{\frac{d+\varsigma}{N_{f}}}\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I_{\gamma}+4\left(\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I_{\gamma}\right)^{2}\\
&\quad+512\sqrt{2}C_{1}^{4}C^{3}\left(\frac{d+\varsigma}{N_{f}}\right)^{\frac{5}{2}}+\frac{128C_{1}^{4}(d+\varsigma)}{N_{f}}\left(\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I_{\gamma}\right)^{3}\\
&\leq C_{5}\frac{d+\varsigma}{N_{f}}+C_{5}\left(\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I_{\gamma}\right)^{2}+\frac{C_{5}(d+\varsigma)}{N_{f}}\left(\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I_{\gamma}\right)^{3},\end{split}

(S.36)

where
C_{5}
does not depend on sample size.
Now, defining a new process,
\bar{\bm{B}}(\bm{\delta})
, such that

\displaystyle\bar{B}(\bm{\delta})=\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})-\mathbb{E}\left\{\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})\right\}

\displaystyle=\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{N_{f}}\left\{\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})+\rho n_{\Xi}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta})-\rho n_{\Xi}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle\quad-\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{N_{f}}\left\{n_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}_{0})+\rho n_{\Xi}\mathbb{E}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta})-\rho n_{\Xi}\mathbb{E}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\bm{\beta}_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{n_{\mathcal{T}}}{N_{f}}\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\{\frac{1}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\bm{x}_{t}\bm{x}_{t}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\right\}\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{\delta}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}\left(\bm{\beta}_{0}+\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{\delta}\right)-\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}\left(\bm{\beta}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}\left(\bm{\beta}_{0}+\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{\delta}\right)\right\}+\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\nabla\mathcal{L}^{\pi}\left(\bm{\beta}_{0}\right)\right\}.

Thus, we have
\bar{\bm{B}}(\bm{0})=\bm{0}
,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\bar{\bm{B}}(\bm{\delta})\right\}=\bm{0}
, and for every

\bm{u},\bm{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{\kappa\sqrt{N}_{\rho}\bm{u}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{\delta}}\bar{\bm{B}}(\bm{\delta})\bm{v}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=1}^{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}\left\{(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t})(\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t})-\mathbb{E}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t})(\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t})\right\}}\prod_{e=1}^{n_{\Xi}}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}\left\{\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})(\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e})-\rho\mathbb{E}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}})(\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}})\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{t=1}^{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\left[1+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}|^{2}|\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}|^{2}+\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2}\right\}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}|+\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{e=1}^{n_{\Xi}}\left[1+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\rho^{2}\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}^{2}}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|^{2}|\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|^{2}+\rho\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2}\right\}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}\left(\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|+\rho\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{t=1}^{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\left[1+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}|^{2}|\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}|^{2}+\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2}\right\}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}|+\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{e=1}^{n_{\Xi}}\Big[1+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\pi_{e}\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\rho^{2}}{\pi_{e}^{2}}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|^{2}|\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|^{2}+\rho\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2}\right\}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}\left(\frac{\rho}{\pi_{e}}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|+\rho\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}(1-\pi_{e})\rho\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}\rho\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{t=1}^{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\left[1+e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}|)}+e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}|^{2}|\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}|^{2}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}|)}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{e=1}^{n_{\Xi}}\Big[1+e^{\frac{\rho|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\rho\pi_{e}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{c_{\pi}\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|)}+e^{\frac{\rho|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\frac{\rho}{c_{\pi}}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|^{2}|\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|^{2}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\bm{v}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}|)}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+e^{\frac{\rho|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}(1-\pi_{e})\rho\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{t=1}^{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\left[1+e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\max_{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{w}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2})}+e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\max_{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\bm{w}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{4}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{w}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2})}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\Xi}}\Big[1+e^{\frac{\rho|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{\rho\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\max_{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\cup\bm{0}}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{c_{\pi}\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{w}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2})}+e^{\frac{\rho|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{\rho\kappa^{2}}{c_{\pi}N_{f}}\max_{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{4}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2})}\right\}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{n_{\mathcal{T}}\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\max_{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{w}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2})}+e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{n_{\mathcal{T}}\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\max_{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\bm{w}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{4}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{w}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2})}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp\Big[e^{\frac{\rho|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}\kappa^{2}}{N_{f}}\max_{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\cup\bm{0}}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{c_{\pi}\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{w}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2})}+e^{\frac{\rho|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}\kappa^{2}}{c_{\pi}N_{f}}\max_{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{4}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2})}\right\}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big[e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{c_{\pi}\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\kappa^{2}\frac{1}{c_{\pi}}\max_{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\cup\bm{0}}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{c_{\pi}\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{w}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2})}+e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{c_{\pi}\sqrt{N_{f}}}}\kappa^{2}\frac{1}{c_{\pi}}\max_{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{4}e^{\frac{|\kappa|}{c_{\pi}\sqrt{N_{f}}}(|\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}|^{2})}\right\}\Big]

Therefore, there exists constants
C_{6},C_{7}>0
depending only on
C_{0}
such that
for any
|\kappa|\leq\sqrt{N_{f}/C_{6}}
,
\sup_{\bm{u},\bm{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}e^{\kappa\sqrt{N}\bm{u}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{\delta}}\bar{\bm{B}}(\bm{\delta})\bm{v}}\leq e^{C_{7}^{2}\kappa^{2}/(2c_{\pi})}
.
Applying Theorem A.3 in
Spokoiny 2013
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(R)}\|\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})-\mathbb{E}\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})\|_{2}\geq 6C_{7}(8d+2\varsigma)^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{\pi}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{f}^{-\frac{1}{2}}R\right\}\leq e^{-\varsigma},

as long as
N_{f}\geq C_{6}(8d+2t)
, which implies that

\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(R)}\|\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})-\mathbb{E}\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{8}(d+\varsigma)}{N_{f}}+\sqrt{\frac{C_{8}(d+\varsigma)}{N_{f}}}\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I_{\gamma}\leq\frac{3}{2}\frac{C_{8}(d+\varsigma)}{N_{f}}+\left(\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}I_{\gamma}\right)^{2}

(S.37)

where
C_{8}=24C_{7}C
with probability at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}
at least

1-e^{-\varsigma}
. Thus, combining (
S.35
), (
S.36
),
and (
S.37
). we have that

\sup_{\bm{\beta}\in\Theta_{0}(r)}\|\bm{B}(\bm{\beta})\|_{2}\leq C_{9}\frac{\rho n_{\Xi}}{N_{f}}\check{I}_{\gamma}+\frac{C_{9}(d+\varsigma)}{N_{f}},

where
\check{I}_{\gamma}=I_{\gamma}+\rho n_{\Xi}N_{f}^{-1}(I_{\gamma})^{2}+(d+\varsigma)(\rho n_{\Xi})^{2}N_{f}^{-3}(I_{\gamma})^{3}
. This complete the proof.

∎

Proof of Corollary
1
(II)
.

To prove Corollary
1
(II), first notice that

\mathbb{E}\{\mathrm{vec}(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top})-\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\Sigma})\}=\bm{0}
. Since

\mathrm{vec}(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top})=\tilde{\bm{x}}\otimes\tilde{\bm{x}}
, we have that

\mathbb{V}\{\mathrm{vec}(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top})\}=\mathbb{E}\{\mathrm{vec}(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top})\mathrm{vec}(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top})^{\top}\}-\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\Sigma})\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\Sigma})^{\top}=\mathbb{E}\{(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top})\otimes(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top})\}-\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\Sigma})\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\Sigma})^{\top}
and
\|\mathbb{V}\{\mathrm{vec}(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top})\}\|_{F}\leq\mathbb{E}\{\|(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top})\otimes(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top})\|_{F}\}=\mathbb{E}\{\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|^{4}\}
. Therefore,
taking

\bm{W}_{t}=n_{\mathcal{T}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{V}\{\mathrm{vec}(\tilde{\bm{x}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top})\}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\{\mathrm{vec}(\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top})-\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\Sigma})\}
,
we have that
\mathbb{E}(\bm{W}_{t})=\bm{0}
and
\mathbb{V}(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}W_{t})=\bm{I}_{d\times d}
. Applying
Theorem 1.1 in
Raič 2019
, we have that
