\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\bm{W}_{t}\right\|_{2}>\sqrt{\varsigma+d}\right)-\mathbb{P}(\|\bm{Z}\|_{2}>\sqrt{\varsigma})\leq\frac{C\sqrt{d}\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2}^{6})}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}.

Thus, we know that with probability at least
1-e^{-\varsigma}-C/\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\right\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|^{4}}\sqrt{\varsigma+d}\lesssim\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sqrt{d+\varsigma}.

Using the same argument, we can also obtain that with probability at least

1-e^{-\varsigma}-C/\sqrt{r}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\right\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{\rho n_{\Xi}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|^{4}}\sqrt{\varsigma+d}\lesssim\sqrt{r}\sqrt{d+\varsigma}.

Therefore, with probability at least

1-Ce^{-\varsigma}-C/\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}
,

\left\|\bm{V}_{\lambda}-\bm{\Sigma}\right\|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d+\varsigma}{N_{f\lambda}}}.

Combining with the fact that

\frac{1}{N_{f\lambda}}\left\{\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\varepsilon_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{e\in\Xi}w_{e}\varepsilon_{e}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{e}\right\}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}(d+\varsigma)}}{N_{f\lambda}}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\frac{\sqrt{r(d+\varsigma)}}{N_{f}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{d+\varsigma}{N_{f\lambda}}},

we complete the proof.

∎

Proof of Proposition
2
.

We first prove that
\psi(z;\lambda)
is an odd function of
z
as long as

P(\cdot)
is an even penalty function. This is true due to the definition of

\psi(z;\lambda)
. Since

\Theta(z;\lambda)=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\gamma\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{(z-\gamma)^{2}+\lambda P(\gamma)\right\}^{2}
, we have that

\Theta(-z;\lambda)=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\gamma\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{(-z-\gamma)^{2}+\lambda P(\gamma)\right\}^{2}=\mathop{\arg\min}_{\gamma\in\mathbb{R}}\left[\left\{z-(-\gamma)\right\}^{2}+\lambda P(-\gamma)\right]^{2}=-\Theta(z;\lambda).

Therefore, we have that

\psi(-z;\lambda)=-z-\Theta(-z;\lambda)=-z+\Theta(z;\lambda)=-\psi(z;\lambda)
. Thus,

\psi(z;\lambda)
is odd as long as
P(\cdot)
is even. Therefore, it is easy to
know that the
\psi_{\nu}(\varepsilon;\lambda)
is odd for both
\nu=1,2
, where

\psi_{\nu}(z;\lambda)
represents the
\psi(z;\lambda)
function corresponding
to
\ell_{1}
or
\ell_{2}
penalties discussed in the main paper.
Since
\varepsilon_{e}
is symmetric and
\psi_{\nu}(\varepsilon;\lambda)
is an odd
function of
\varepsilon
, we have that

\mathbb{E}\left\{\psi_{\nu}(\varepsilon;\lambda)\right\}=0
, and thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{\nu}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{e}\right\|^{2}\Big|\bm{X}_{\Xi}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{e\in\Xi}\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\rho^{2}\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}^{2}}\psi_{\nu}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)^{2}\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{x}_{e}\Big|\bm{X}_{\Xi}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n_{\Xi}}^{n_{\Xi}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\rho^{2}\delta_{i}\delta_{j}}{\pi_{i}\pi_{j}}\psi_{\nu}(\varepsilon_{i};\lambda)\psi_{\nu}(\varepsilon_{j};\lambda)\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{x}_{j}\Big|\bm{X}_{\Xi}\right\}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\mathbb{E}\left\{\psi_{\nu}(\varepsilon;\lambda)\right\}^{2}\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\bm{x}_{e}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{x}_{e}}{\pi_{e}}

Let
t_{e}=\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{e}\|_{2}^{2}
.
Minimizing
\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{\rho\delta_{e}}{\pi_{e}}\psi_{\nu}(\varepsilon_{e};\lambda)\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{e}\right\|^{2}\Big|\bm{X}_{\Xi}\right\}
is equivalent to minimizing

\sum_{e\in\Xi}\frac{t_{e}^{2}}{\pi_{e}},

subject to
\sum_{e\in\Xi}\pi_{e}=r
and
0\leq\pi_{e}\leq 1
for any

e\in\Xi
. Following the same proof of Theorem 4 in
Wang et al. 2022
with

t_{(i)}=\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{e}\|_{(i),2}^{2}
, we obtain that

\pi_{e}^{{\mathrm{opt}}}=\frac{r\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{e}\|_{2}\wedge H}{\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\Xi}}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}\wedge H},

where

H=\frac{r\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\Xi}-g}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}\|_{(i)}}{r-g},

\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}\|_{(1)}\leq\ldots\leq\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}\|_{(n_{\Xi})}

are order statistics of

\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{1}\|,\ldots,\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{n_{\Xi}}\|
, and
g

is an integer such that

\frac{r\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}\|_{(n_{\Xi}-g)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\Xi}-g}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}\|_{(i)}}<\frac{r}{r-g},

and

\frac{r\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}\|_{(n_{\Xi}-g+1)}}{\sum_{b=1}^{n_{\Xi}-g+1}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}\|_{(b)}}\geq\frac{r}{r-g+1},

in which we define
\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}\|_{(n_{\Xi}+1)}=\infty
.
∎

A.4
Mathematical details of Section
4

In this section, our goal is to prove Proposition
3
and
Theorem
3
. Since in this section, we mainly focus on the external
data, therefore, without special illustration, we always use
\varepsilon
to
represent the random error of the external data and use
\bm{x}
to represent the
covariate variable of the external data. For a random variable
Y
, we use notation
Y_{(k)}

to represent its order statistics, and for a random variable
X
that is related
to
Y
, we use notation
X_{[k]}
to represent the corresponding concomitant of
order statistics
Y_{(k)}
. For example, we selected data points with order
statistics
|y-\bm{x}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}|_{(1)},|y-\bm{x}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}|_{(2)},\ldots|y-\bm{x}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}|_{(r)}
, and therefore, the selected data points can be
represented as concomitants

(\varepsilon_{[1]},\bm{x}_{[1]}^{\top},\gamma_{[1]})^{\top},(\varepsilon_{[2]},\bm{x}_{[2]}^{\top},\gamma_{[2]})^{\top},\ldots,(\varepsilon_{[r]},\bm{x}_{[r]}^{\top},\gamma_{[r]})^{\top}
. To ease the notation, we denote

\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}=\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top})^{-1}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\varepsilon_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}
in this
section and denote
Z^{0}=\varepsilon-n_{\mathcal{T}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}
and

Z^{\gamma}=\varepsilon-n_{\mathcal{T}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}+\gamma
. Note that

\displaystyle y-\bm{x}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}=\varepsilon+\gamma+\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{0}-\bm{x}^{\top}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\bm{x}_{t}\bm{x}_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\varepsilon_{t}\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{0}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon+\gamma+\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{0}-\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\varepsilon_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{0}=Z^{\gamma}.

We first prove Proposition
3
in Section
A.4.1
.

A.4.1
Proof of Proposition
3

We prove Proposition
3
and start with a lemma.

Lemma S.5
.

Under Assumptions
2
,
4
, and
5
, we have
that
\forall 1\leq r\leq n_{\Xi}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|Z^{\gamma}|_{(r)}>\frac{r+\sqrt{r\varsigma}}{n_{\Xi}}\Big|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)\lesssim e^{-\varsigma}+r^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}\wedge 1},\text{ almost surely},

and for any function
h(z)
that satisfies
\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}|h(z)|\leq 1
,
we have that for any Borel set
\mathcal{A}
,

\left|\int_{\mathcal{A}}h(z)\mathrm{d}F_{n_{\Xi}}^{(r)}(z)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{r!}\int_{\mathcal{A}}|h(z)|z^{r}e^{-z}\mathrm{d}z+r^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}\wedge 1},\text{ almost surely},

(S.38)

where
F_{n_{\Xi}}^{(r)}
is the c.d.f. of
n_{\Xi}|Z^{\gamma}|_{(r)}
conditional on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}
.

Proof.

We have that the density of
|Z^{\gamma}|
conditional on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}
is given as

\displaystyle f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}f_{\varepsilon}\left(sz-\gamma+\frac{u}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right)f_{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}(u)f_{\gamma}(\gamma)\mathrm{d}\gamma\mathrm{d}u.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(0)=1
in this proof, since
we can always consider
Z^{\gamma}=\kappa(\varepsilon+\gamma-n_{\mathcal{T}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}})
,
where
\kappa=f_{|\varepsilon+\gamma-n_{\mathcal{T}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}|}(0)
, so
that
f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(0)=1
. We first prove that the c.d.f of
n_{\Xi}|Z^{\gamma}|
conditional
on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}
, denoted as
F_{n_{\Xi}}^{(r)}
, converges to a
degenerate distribution. This result can be prove with results of extreme
statistics of i.d.d. data points since
Z^{\gamma}_{1},Z^{\gamma}_{2},\ldots,Z^{\gamma}_{n_{\Xi}}
are i.i.d.
data points conditional on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}
. Letting
\xi=1/|Z^{\gamma}|
, we have that

\xi_{(i)}=1/|Z^{\gamma}|_{(n_{\Xi}-i+1)}
,

F_{\xi}(z)=1-F_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(z^{-1})
and
f_{\xi}(z)=z^{-2}f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(z^{-1})
. We
now prove that
F_{\xi}
satisfies VM1 in
Falk and Marohn 1993
. Since

\omega(F_{\xi})=\sup\{z\in\mathbb{R}:F_{\xi}(z)<1\}=\infty
and

f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(0)=1
, we only need to show that as
z\to\infty
,

\displaystyle f_{\xi}(z)=\frac{1}{z^{2}}f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}\left(\frac{1}{z}\right)=\frac{1}{z^{2}}\left\{1+f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}\left(\frac{1}{z}\right)-f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(0)\right\}=\frac{1}{z^{2}}\left\{1+Cz^{-\alpha_{\varepsilon}}\right\},

where
C
is a fixed constant that does not depend on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}
, which will verify
VM1 in
Falk and Marohn 1993
. Under Assumption
5
,
applying dominating convergence theorem, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{z\to\infty}z^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}}\left\{f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}\left(\frac{1}{z}\right)-f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(0)\right\}=\lim_{w\to 0}\frac{f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(w)-f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(0)}{w^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{w\to 0}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f_{\varepsilon}\left(w-\gamma+\frac{u}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right)-f_{\varepsilon}\left(-\gamma+\frac{u}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right)}{w^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}}}f_{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}(u)f_{\gamma}(\gamma)\mathrm{d}\gamma\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad+\lim_{w\to 0}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f_{\varepsilon}\left(-w-\gamma+\frac{u}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right)-f_{\varepsilon}\left(-\gamma+\frac{u}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right)}{w^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}}}f_{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}(u)f_{\gamma}(\gamma)\mathrm{d}\gamma\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\left\{H\left(-\gamma+\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right)\Big|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right\}\leq C_{1},

where the last inequality is due to Assumption
5
, which implies
that
H(\cdot)
is bounded by a constant almost surely.
Therefore, we have that

f_{\xi}(z)=z^{-2}\left\{1+O(z^{-\alpha_{\varepsilon}})\right\}
. Due to Theorem
2.4 and Theorem 3.1 in
Falk and Marohn 1993
, we have that for any
Borel set
\mathcal{A}
and
r\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{\xi_{(n-r+1)}}{n_{\Xi}}\in\mathcal{A}\Big|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right\}-\int_{\mathcal{A}}g_{r+1}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right|\leq C_{2}\left\{\left(\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}}r^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right\},\text{ almost surely},

where
g_{r+1}(z)=\left\{-\log G(z)\right\}^{r}g(z)/r!
,

G(z)=e^{-\frac{1}{z}}
, and
C_{2}
is a constant that only determined by
C_{1}
. Here,
g_{r+1}(z)
is the asymptotic distribution of
extreme statistics that satisfies condition VM1 in
Falk and Marohn 1993
. The
explicit formula of
g_{r+1}(z)
and
G(z)
is given
in
Nagaraja and David 1994
. The fact that
C_{2}
is determined by
C_{1}

is because of the proof in
Falk and Marohn 1993
. Therefore, we have that for any Borel set

\mathcal{A}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left\{n_{\Xi}|Z^{\gamma}|_{(r)}\in\mathcal{A}\Big|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right\}-\frac{1}{r!}\int_{\mathcal{A}}z^{r}e^{-z}\mathrm{d}z\right|\lesssim r^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}\wedge 1},\text{ almost surely}.

Now, letting
\mathcal{A}=((r+\sqrt{r\varsigma})/n_{\Xi},\infty)
and denoting

\Gamma_{r}
as a gamma distributed random variable with shape parameter
r
and
scale parameter 1, we have that
\forall 1\leq r\leq n_{\Xi}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(|Z^{\gamma}|_{(r)}>\frac{r+\sqrt{r\varsigma}}{n_{\Xi}}\Big|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{r!}\int_{r+\sqrt{r\varsigma}}^{\infty}z^{r}e^{-z}\mathrm{d}z+r^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}\wedge 1}\\
&\lesssim\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\Gamma_{r}}{r}-1>\sqrt{\frac{\varsigma}{r}}\right)+r^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}\wedge 1}\\
&\lesssim e^{-r\frac{\varsigma}{r}}+r^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}\wedge 1}=e^{-\varsigma}+r^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}\wedge 1},\end{split}

almost surely. Further, we have that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathcal{A}}h(z)\mathrm{d}F_{n_{\Xi}}^{(r)}(z)\right|\leq\frac{1}{r!}\int_{\mathcal{A}}|h(z)|z^{r}e^{-z}\mathrm{d}z+\left|\mathbb{P}\left\{n_{\Xi}|Z^{\gamma}|_{(r)}\in\mathcal{A}\right\}-\int_{\mathcal{A}}z^{r}e^{-z}\mathrm{d}z\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{r!}\int_{\mathcal{A}}|h(z)|z^{r}e^{-z}\mathrm{d}z+r^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}\wedge 1},

almost surely, which completes the proof of Lemma
S.5
.
∎

Now, we present the proof of Proposition
3
.

Proof of Proposition
3
.

Letting

\displaystyle g_{\varepsilon}(t)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f_{\varepsilon}\left(t+\frac{u}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right)f_{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}(u)\mathrm{d}u\text{, and }g_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}(t)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}\left(t+\frac{u}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right)f_{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}(u)\mathrm{d}u,

since
f_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)
is a Lipschitz function with a Lipschitz constant
L_{\varepsilon}
,
we know that
\forall t\in\mathbb{R}
, condtional on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}
,

\displaystyle|g_{\varepsilon}(t)-f_{\varepsilon}(0)|\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|f_{\varepsilon}\left(t+\frac{u}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right)-f_{\varepsilon}(0)\right|f_{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}(u)\mathrm{d}u\leq L_{\varepsilon}|t|+\frac{L_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2})\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}.

Since under Assumption
5
, we have that
f_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}
is also a
Lipschitz function with Lipschitz constant
L_{\varepsilon}
because

\displaystyle\left|f_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}(z+w)-f_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}(z)\right|\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|f_{\varepsilon}(z+w-\tilde{\gamma})-f_{\varepsilon}(z-\tilde{\gamma})\right|f_{\tilde{\gamma}}(\tilde{\gamma})\mathrm{d}\tilde{\gamma}\leq L_{\varepsilon}|w|.

Therefore, we similarly have that
\forall t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle|g_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}(t)-f_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}(0)|\leq L_{\varepsilon}|t|+\frac{L_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2})\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}.

Now, we consider a “good” set
\mathcal{A}
, such that on
\mathcal{A}
, we
have that
\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\varsigma
. Then, if
\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\geq 4C\{f_{\varepsilon}(0)\}^{-1}L_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2})\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}\sqrt{\varsigma}
, we
have that
\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\geq 4\{f_{\varepsilon}(0)\}^{-1}L_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2})\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}
.
On “good” set
\mathcal{A}
, we have that applying Bayesian’s rule,
\forall 0<z<\frac{f_{\varepsilon}(0)}{8L_{\varepsilon}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma>0\big||Z^{\gamma}|=z,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)=\frac{f_{\left|\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}-\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right|}(z)\mathbb{P}(\gamma\neq 0)}{f_{\left|\varepsilon-\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right|}(z)\mathbb{P}(\gamma=0)+f_{\left|\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}-\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right|}(z)\mathbb{P}(\gamma\neq 0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{\gamma}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}g_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}(sz)}{(1-p_{\gamma})\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}g_{\varepsilon}(sz)+p_{\gamma}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}g_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}(sz)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p_{\gamma}}{1-p_{\gamma}}\frac{f_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}(0)+L_{\varepsilon}|z|+\frac{L_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2})\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}}{f_{\varepsilon}(0)-L_{\varepsilon}|z|-\frac{L_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}(\|\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{2})\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}}

\displaystyle\leq Cp_{\gamma}\left\{f_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}(0)+z+\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right\},

where the last inequality is because
p_{\gamma}
is upper bounded away from 1
and
C
is a constant that does not depend on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}
.
Therefore, using the same arguments in
Nagaraja and David 1994
to obtain
(2.1), we can obtain that
\forall 0<z_{0}<\frac{f_{\varepsilon}(0)}{8L_{\varepsilon}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq r}\gamma_{[k]}=0\Big||Z^{\gamma}|_{(r+1)}=z_{0},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)=\left\{\int_{0}^{z_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma=0\Big||Z^{\gamma}|=z\right)\frac{f_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(z)}{F_{|Z^{\gamma}|}(z_{0})}\mathrm{d}z\right\}^{r}

\displaystyle\geq\left[1-Cp_{\gamma}\left\{f_{\varepsilon+\gamma}(0)+z_{0}+\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right\}\right]^{r}\geq 1-Crp_{\gamma}\left\{f_{\varepsilon+\gamma}(0)+z_{0}+\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right\}.

Thus, due to (
S.38
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq r}\gamma_{[k]}>0\Big|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{(r+1)!}\int_{0}^{\frac{n_{\Xi}f_{\varepsilon}(0)}{8L_{\varepsilon}}}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq r}\gamma_{[k]}>0\Big||Z^{\gamma}|_{(r+1)}=\frac{z}{n_{\Xi}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)z^{r+1}e^{-z}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{(r+1)!}\int_{\frac{n_{\Xi}f_{\varepsilon}(0)}{8L_{\varepsilon}}}^{\infty}z^{r+1}e^{-z}\mathrm{d}z+(r+1)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{r+1}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}\wedge 1}

\displaystyle\lesssim rp_{\gamma}f_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}(0)+\frac{rp_{\gamma}\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}+\frac{rp_{\gamma}}{n_{\Xi}}\frac{1}{(r+1)!}\int_{0}^{\infty}z^{r+2}e^{-z}\mathrm{d}z+\mathbb{P}\left(\Gamma_{r+1}>\frac{n_{\Xi}f_{\varepsilon}(0)}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+r^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}\wedge 1}

\displaystyle\lesssim rp_{\gamma}f_{\varepsilon+\tilde{\gamma}}(0)+\frac{rp_{\gamma}\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}+\frac{r^{2}p_{\gamma}}{n_{\Xi}}+\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}+r^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right)^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}\wedge 1}:=\check{p}

where
\Gamma_{r+1}
represent a Gamma distributed random variable with shape
parameter
r+1
and scale parameter 1. Therefore, we know that on “good” set

\mathcal{A}
, we have that

\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma_{[i]}=0,k=1,\ldots,r|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)\geq 1-C\check{p}

Now, we know that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma_{[i]}=0,k=1,\ldots,r\right)\geq\int_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma_{[i]}=0,k=1,\ldots,r\big|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}\geq(1-C\check{p})\{1-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{c}))\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-C\check{p}-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{c}).

Now, since “good” set
\mathcal{A}
satisfies with probability at least

1-\frac{C}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}
according to Lemma
S.1
, we complete the
proof.

∎

A.4.2
Proof of Theorem
3

In the following, our proofs are based on normal assumptions such that

\varepsilon\sim\mathbb{N}(0,\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})
and
\bm{x}\sim\mathbb{N}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma})
.
To ease the
notations, we denote
\bm{z}_{f}=(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})^{\top}
,
\bm{z}_{c}=(z_{1},z_{2})^{\top}
,

\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}=\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\frac{\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}
,

\displaystyle\bm{\mu}_{c}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\\
-\frac{\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\\
\end{pmatrix},\bm{\Sigma}_{f}=\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}&0&\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\\
0&1&-\frac{\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\\
\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}&-\frac{\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}&\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}\\
\end{pmatrix},

and

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{c}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\frac{\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}&\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\\
\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}&1-\frac{1}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\frac{\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\\
\end{pmatrix},

where
\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
represent any vector with norm equals to 1 in

\mathbb{R}^{d}
in this section. To prove Theorem
3
, we start with
some lemmas.

Lemma S.6
.

Under Assumption
4
and assume that

\varepsilon\sim\mathbb{N}(0,\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})
and
\bm{x}\sim\mathbb{N}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma})
, we
have that
\forall\varsigma>0,0<c_{0}<8
, as long as
n_{\mathcal{T}}\gtrsim e^{2\varsigma}
,

r\leq\{1-(1+c_{0})p_{\gamma}\}n_{\Xi}
, and
\gamma_{b}\geq 4\sqrt{6}\sigma_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}
, we have that

\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma_{[1]}=\ldots\gamma_{[r]}=0\right)\geq 1-Crp_{\gamma}e^{\frac{-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}-Ce^{-C\{(c_{0}^{2}p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\wedge\log n_{\Xi}\}},

where
C
is a constant the does not depend on any sample sizes.

Proof.

Under normal assumptions, it is easy to know that given any
u\geq 0
, random
variable
\varepsilon+u-\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}
is
normally distributed and the density function of
random variable
\varepsilon+u-\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\Xi}}}
given
u\geq 0
is

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon+u-\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}}(z)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{Z^{0}}}e^{-\frac{(z-u)^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}}.

Thus, applying Bayesian’s rule, we have that
\forall z>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma=0\big||Z^{\gamma}|=z,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)=\frac{\mathbb{P}(\gamma=0)f_{\left|\varepsilon-\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right|}(z)}{\mathbb{P}(\gamma=0)f_{\left|\varepsilon-\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right|}(z)+\mathbb{P}(\gamma\neq 0)\int_{\gamma_{b}}^{\infty}f_{\left|\varepsilon+u-\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right|}(z)f_{\tilde{\gamma}}(u)\mathrm{d}u}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-p_{\gamma})\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{Z^{0}}}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}}}{(1-p_{\gamma})\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{Z^{0}}}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}}+p_{\gamma}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{Z^{0}}}\int_{\gamma_{b}}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{(sz-u)^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}}f_{\tilde{\gamma}}(u)\mathrm{d}u}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{\frac{1}{2}p_{\gamma}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{\gamma_{b}}^{\infty}e^{\frac{2szu-u^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}}f_{\tilde{\gamma}}(u)\mathrm{d}u}{1-p_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}p_{\gamma}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{\gamma_{b}}^{\infty}e^{\frac{2szu-u^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}}f_{\tilde{\gamma}}(u)\mathrm{d}u}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{p_{\gamma}}{1-p_{\gamma}}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}\int_{\gamma_{b}}^{\infty}\exp\left\{\frac{2szu-u^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right\}f_{\tilde{\gamma}}(u)\mathrm{d}u\geq 1-\frac{p_{\gamma}}{1-p_{\gamma}}\exp\left\{\frac{2z\gamma_{b}-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right\}.

The last inequality is because

\exp\left(\frac{-2zu-u^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)\leq\exp\left(\frac{2zu-u^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)
. Now,
letting

K_{\gamma}:=\#\{\gamma_{i}\neq 0,i=1,2,\ldots,n_{\Xi}\}
,
we consider a “good” set
\mathcal{A}
that satisfies the following
conditions:

\displaystyle K_{\gamma}\leq n_{\Xi}-r,\text{ }\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\text{, and}\max_{1\leq i\leq n_{\Xi}}|Z^{0}|_{i}\leq 2\sigma_{Z^{0}}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}.

(S.39)

Under “good” set
\mathcal{A}
, using similar argument of
Claim

(3.3.1) in
Oliveira et al. 2025
, we can obtain that
\forall i\in\{1,2,\ldots,r\}
,

\displaystyle|Z^{\gamma}|_{(i)}\leq|Z^{0}|_{(i+K_{\gamma})}\leq 2\sqrt{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}\leq\sigma_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{6\log n_{\Xi}}.

Thus, we know that when
\gamma_{b}\geq 4\sqrt{6}\sigma_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}
, applying Lemma 3.3.1
in
Bhattacharya 1984
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma_{[1]}=\ldots\gamma_{[r]}=0\right)=\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma_{[1]}=\ldots\gamma_{[r]}=0|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}},|Z^{\gamma}|_{(1)},\ldots|Z^{\gamma}|_{(n_{\Xi})}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma_{[1]}=\ldots\gamma_{[r]}=0|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}},|Z^{\gamma}|_{(1)},\ldots|Z^{\gamma}|_{(n_{\Xi})}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathcal{A}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}\left[1-\frac{p_{\gamma}}{1-p_{\gamma}}\exp\left\{\frac{2|Z^{\gamma}|_{(i)}\gamma_{b}-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right\}\right]\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\geq\left\{1-\frac{p_{\gamma}}{1-p_{\gamma}}\exp\left(\frac{2\sqrt{6}\sigma_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}\gamma_{b}-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{2\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)\right\}^{r}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle\geq\left\{1-\frac{p_{\gamma}}{1-p_{\gamma}}\exp\left(\frac{\frac{\gamma_{b}}{2}-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{2\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)\right\}^{r}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A})=\left(1-\frac{p_{\gamma}}{1-p_{\gamma}}e^{\frac{-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}\right)^{r}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{p_{\gamma}}{1-p_{\gamma}}e^{\frac{-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}\right)^{r}\left\{1-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{c})\right\}\geq\left(1-\frac{p_{\gamma}}{1-p_{\gamma}}e^{\frac{-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}\right)^{r}-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{c}).

Now, we only need to calculate the probability of
\mathcal{A}^{c}
.
Note that
I(\gamma_{i}\neq 0),i=1,2,\ldots,n_{\Xi}
is a Bernoulli random variable
with probability
p_{\gamma}
and therefore,
K_{\gamma}=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\Xi}}I(\gamma_{i}\neq 0)
is a binomial random
variable. Using Theorem 3 in
Okamoto 1959
, we have that
\forall c>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{K_{\gamma}>n_{\Xi}(\sqrt{p_{\gamma}}+\sqrt{c})^{2}\right\}=\mathbb{P}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\Xi}}I(\gamma_{i}\neq 0)>n_{\Xi}(\sqrt{p_{\gamma}}+\sqrt{c})^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{Binomial(n_{\Xi},p_{\gamma})}{n_{\Xi}}}>\sqrt{p_{\gamma}}+\sqrt{c}\right\}<e^{-2cn_{\Xi}}.

Taking
c=\frac{c_{0}^{2}p_{\gamma}}{16}
, we have that with probability at least

1-e^{-\frac{c_{0}^{2}p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi}}{8}}
,

K_{\gamma}\leq p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi}\left(1+\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{16}+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\right)<(1+c_{0})p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi},

(S.40)

where the last inequality is because
c_{0}<8
. Therefore, as long as

r\leq\{1-(1+c_{0})p_{\gamma}\}n_{\Xi}
, we have that

K_{\gamma}\leq n_{\Xi}-r
with probability at least
1-e^{-\frac{c_{0}^{2}p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi}}{8}}
. We also have
that with probability at least
1-\frac{C}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}
,

\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\varsigma
. Therefore, when
n_{\mathcal{T}}\geq 2C\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\varsigma/\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}
, we know that

\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\varepsilon^{2}}
with
probability at least
1-C/\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}
. Finally, since
Z^{0}_{i}
,

i=1,2,3,\ldots,n_{\Xi}
, are normal distributed with variances
\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}
,
which implies
\forall\varsigma_{1}>0
,
\max_{1\leq i\leq n_{\Xi}}|Z^{0}|_{i}\leq\sigma_{Z^{0}}\sqrt{2(\log n_{\Xi}+\varsigma_{1})}
with probability at
least
1-e^{-\varsigma_{1}}
. Taking
\varsigma_{1}=\log n_{\Xi}
, we have that
\max_{1\leq i\leq n_{\Xi}}|Z^{0}|_{i}\leq 2\sigma_{Z^{0}}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}
with
probability at least
1-n_{\Xi}^{-1}
, Thus, we have that

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{c})\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}+e^{-\frac{c_{0}^{2}p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi}}{8}}+\frac{1}{n_{\Xi}},

which completes the proof.

∎

We highlight the following corollary obtained from the proof of
Lemma
S.6
. Assume that we sort
|Z^{0}|_{e}
,

e=1,\ldots,n_{\Xi}
, such that

|Z^{0}|_{(1)}<|Z^{0}|_{(2)}<\cdots<|Z^{0}|_{(n_{\Xi})}
. Define
i[k]
to be the index

j
such that
|Z^{\gamma}|_{(k)}=|Z^{0}|_{(j)}
when
\gamma=0
. Our definition of index

i[k]
can be interpreted as follows. Since

|Z^{\gamma}|=\varepsilon+\gamma-\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}
can be considered as
a contaminated version of clean sample

|Z^{0}|=\varepsilon-\frac{\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}
. The order statistics of
contaminated samples should correspond to a clean sample in the original clean
samples. Therefore, the index
i[k]
tells us which clean sample (in the sense
of order statistics) is the
k
-th ordered contaminated sample
|Z^{\gamma}|_{(k)}

corresponding to.

Corollary 3
.

Under the same Assumptions in Lemma
S.6
, we further have that with
probability at least

1-Crp_{\gamma}e^{\frac{-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}-Ce^{-C\{(c_{0}^{2}p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\wedge\log n_{\Xi}\}}
,
we have that
\forall k=1,2,\ldots,r

|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}=|Z^{\gamma}|_{(k)}\leq 2\sigma_{Z^{0}}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}.

Proof.

The result can be derived directly from the fact that under event

\{\gamma_{[1]}=\gamma_{[2]}=\ldots\gamma_{[r]}=0\}\cap\mathcal{A}
, where

\mathcal{A}
is the “good” set defined in the proof of
Lemma
S.6
, we have that

|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}=|Z^{\gamma}|_{(k)}\leq|Z^{0}|_{(k+K_{\gamma})},

(S.41)

and
\max_{1\leq i\leq n_{\Xi}}|Z^{0}|_{i}\leq 2\sigma_{Z^{0}}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}
.
∎

Lemma S.7
.

Under Assumption
4
and assume that

\varepsilon\sim\mathbb{N}(0,\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})
and
\bm{x}\sim\mathbb{N}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma})
,

\forall\varsigma>0,0<c_{0}<8
, as long as
n_{\mathcal{T}}\gtrsim e^{2\varsigma}
,

r\leq\{1-(1+c_{0})p_{\gamma}\}n_{\Xi}
,
\gamma_{b}\geq 4\sqrt{6}\sigma_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}
, and there exists a
constant
0<\alpha_{3}<1
, such that
r\geq 2^{\frac{2}{\alpha_{3}}}
and

r^{\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2}}n_{\mathcal{T}}\gtrsim\varsigma\log n_{\Xi}
, we have that with
probability at least

1-Crp_{\gamma}e^{-\frac{\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}-Ce^{-C\{(c_{0}^{2}p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\wedge\log n_{\Xi}\}}-2e^{-Cr^{1-\alpha_{3}}}
,

\sigma_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{r}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i]}^{\top}\right)\geq\frac{1}{8}.

Proof.

Letting
\mu_{\bm{x},c}=-\frac{\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}
and

\sigma_{\bm{x},c}^{2}=1-\frac{1}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}
, we know
that
\forall\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d}
,

f_{\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{u}}^{|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}},|Z^{0}|=z_{3}}(z)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{\bm{x},c}}e^{-\frac{(z-s\mu_{\bm{x},c}z_{3})^{2}}{2\sigma_{\bm{x},c}^{2}}}.

It is easy to know that

\mathbb{E}\left(\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u}\big||Z^{0}|=z_{3},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)=0
,
and therefore,
\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\lambda\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u}}\big||Z^{0}|=z_{3},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)=\frac{e^{\lambda\mu_{\bm{x},c}z_{3}}+e^{-\lambda\mu_{\bm{x},c}z_{3}}}{2}e^{\frac{\lambda^{2}\sigma_{\bm{x},c}^{2}}{2}}\leq\exp\left\{\frac{\lambda^{2}(\mu_{\bm{x},c}^{2}z_{3}^{2}+\sigma_{\bm{x},c}^{2})}{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{\frac{\lambda^{2}\left(1-\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}+\frac{(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}})^{2}z_{3}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}\right)}{2}\right\}:=e^{\frac{\lambda^{2}K(z_{3})}{2}}.

Therefore, conditional on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}
and
|Z^{0}|=z_{3}
,
\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u}
is a
sub-gaussian random variable and

\mathbb{E}\left\{(\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u})^{2}\big||Z^{0}|=z_{3},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right\}=\mu_{\bm{x},c}^{2}z_{3}^{2}+\sigma_{\bm{x},c}^{2}=1-\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}+\frac{(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}})^{2}z_{3}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}=K(z_{3}).

Now, we know that given
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}
and
|Z^{0}|=z_{3}
,
\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u}
is sub-gaussian with
variance proxy
K(z_{3})
, Applying Lemma 3.1 in
Bhattacharya 1984
and
Hanson-Wright inequality, we have that
\forall t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]})^{2}-\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}K(|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])})\right|>t\Big||Z^{\gamma}|_{(k)},k=1,\ldots,r,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i[k]]})^{2}-\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}K(|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])})\right|>t\Big||Z^{0}|_{(i[k])},k=1,\ldots,r,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left\{-C\min\left(\frac{rt^{2}}{A^{2}},\frac{rt}{A}\right)\right\},

where
C
is a constant that does not dependent on any sample sizes and

A=\max_{1\leq k\leq r}K(Z^{0}_{(i[k])})=1-\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}+\frac{(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}})^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}\max_{1\leq k\leq r}|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}^{2}.

Letting
t=\frac{A}{r^{\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2}}}
and taking expectation on both
sides, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]})^{2}-\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}K(|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])})\right|>\frac{1}{r^{\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2}}}\left(1-\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}+\frac{(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}})^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}\max_{1\leq k\leq r}|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}^{2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i[k]]})^{2}-\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}K(|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])})\right|>t\Big||Z^{0}|_{(i[k])},k=1,\ldots,r,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[2\exp\left\{-C\min\left(r^{1-\alpha_{3}},r^{1-\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2}}\right)\right\}\right]=2e^{-Cr^{1-\alpha_{3}}}.

Therefore, we have that with probability at least
1-2e^{-Cr^{1-\alpha_{3}}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]})^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{1}{r^{\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2}}}\right)\left(1-\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)+\frac{(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}})^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}^{2}-\frac{(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}})^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2}}}\max_{1\leq k\leq r}|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{1}{r^{\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2}}}\right)\left(1-\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)-\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2}}}\max_{1\leq k\leq r}|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}^{2}.

Since
\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\varsigma
and
\max_{1\leq k\leq r}|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}\leq 2\sigma_{Z^{0}}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}
with probability at
least

1-Crp_{\gamma}e^{-\frac{\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}-Ce^{-C\{(c_{0}^{2}p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\wedge\log n_{\Xi}\}}

according to Lemma
S.6
and Corollary
3
, we know
that

\displaystyle\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\varsigma}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2},

\displaystyle\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2}}}\max_{1\leq k\leq r}|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\varsigma}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\frac{4\log n_{\Xi}}{r^{\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2}}}\leq\frac{1}{8},

when
n_{\mathcal{T}}\geq 2C\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\varsigma/\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}
and

r^{\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2}}n_{\mathcal{T}}\geq 32\{\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\varsigma\log n_{\Xi}\}/\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}
. Taking

r\geq 2^{\frac{2}{\alpha_{3}}}
, we obtain that

\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}(\bm{u}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\right)-\frac{1}{8}=\frac{1}{8}.

Therefore, we know that

\sigma_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{r}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i]}^{\top}\right)\geq\frac{1}{8}
.
∎

Next, we prove a lemma of order statistics of
\chi_{1}^{2}
distribution that we
will use later.

Lemma S.8
.

Letting
X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,X_{n}
are i.i.d.
\chi_{1}^{2}
distributed random variables,
we have that
\forall 1\leq r\leq n
, the
order statistics
X_{(1)}<X_{(2)}<\cdots<X_{(r)}
satisfy that

\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{i=1}^{r}(1-X_{(i)})\right|\leq Q(U_{(r+1)})+\frac{4\varsigma}{\sqrt{r}}+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\log n}{3r},

(S.42)

with probability at least
1-2e^{-\frac{\varsigma^{2}}{2}}-n^{-1}
, where

Q(u)=\frac{2\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(u)}\exp\{-\frac{1}{2}F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(u)\}}{\sqrt{2\pi}u},

U_{(r+1)}=F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}(X_{(r+1)})
follows the same distribution as the

r+1
-th order statistics of
\{U_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, where
U_{i}\sim U[0,1]
, and

F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(u)
is the quantile function of
\chi_{1}^{2}

distribution. Further, we have that
Q(u)
is continuous, decreasing,

Q(0)=1
and
Q(1)=0
.

Proof.

Our proof is very similar to the proof of Theorem 5.1.1 in
Oliveira et al. 2025

but slightly different since
r^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{r}X_{(i)}
can be considered as a one-sided
trimmed mean. However, the proof of Theorem 5.1.1 in
Oliveira et al. 2025
only
covers two-sided trimmed mean. Note that we can represent
X_{i}
’s and their
order statistics as
X_{i}=F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(U_{i})
and

X_{(i)}=F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(U_{(i)})
, where
\{U_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d. random
variables distributed as uniform distribution
U[0,1]
. We denote
\Delta_{n,r}=X_{(r+1)}
. Due to
Corollary 3.2.3 in
Oliveira et al. 2025
, we know that conditional on

U_{(r+1)}=b
,
X_{(i)},1\leq i\leq r
are i.i.d. random variables with mean

\mu^{(0,b)}
and standard deviation

\sigma^{(0,b)}
, where
\forall 0<b<1

\displaystyle\mu^{(0,b)}=\mathbb{E}\left\{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(U^{(0,b)})\right\},\{\sigma^{(0,b)}\}^{2}=\mathbb{V}\left\{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(U^{(0,b)})\right\}.

and
U^{(0,b)}
is a uniform random variable over
(0,b)
. Here, our definition
of
\mu^{(0,b)}
and
\sigma^{(0,b)}
are same as
Definition 3.2.2 in
Oliveira et al. 2025
ans similarly to the proof of
Theorem 5.1.1 in
Oliveira et al. 2025
, the random variable in the average,
when centered are bounded by
\Delta_{n,r}
in absolute value. Therefore,
Bernstein’s inequality (Theorem 2.3.1 in
Oliveira et al. 2025
) can be used
conditionally on
U_{(r+1)}
to obtain the following inequality,
\forall\varsigma>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{i=1}^{r}X_{(i)}-\mu^{(0,U_{(r+1)})}\right|>\frac{\varsigma\sigma^{(0,U_{(r+1)})}}{\sqrt{r}}+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\Delta_{n,r}}{12r}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{i=1}^{r}X_{(i)}-\mu^{(0,U_{(r+1)})}\right|>\frac{\varsigma\sigma^{(0,U_{(r+1)})}}{\sqrt{r}}+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\Delta_{n,r}}{12r}\Big|U_{(r+1)}\right\}\right]\leq 2e^{-\frac{\varsigma^{2}}{2}}.

Thus, with probability at least
1-2e^{-\frac{\varsigma^{2}}{2}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{i=1}^{r}(1-X_{(i)})\right|\leq|\mu^{(0,U_{(r+1)})}-1|+\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{i=1}^{r}(X_{(i)}-\mu^{(0,U_{(r+1)})})\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\mu^{(0,U_{(r+1)})}-1|+\frac{\varsigma\sigma^{(0,U_{(r+1)})}}{\sqrt{r}}+\frac{\varsigma^{2}X_{(r+1)}}{12r}.

Our next goal is to bound
|\mu^{(0,U_{(r+1)})}-1|
,

\sigma^{(0,U_{(r+1)})}
, and
X_{(r+1)}
, respectively. We start with
calculating
\mu^{(0,b)}
and
\sigma^{(0,b)}
for every
b\in(0,1)
.
Denoting random variable
F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(U^{(0,b)})=\xi^{2}
, we know that

\mu^{(0,b)}=\mathbb{E}(\xi^{2})
and

\{\sigma^{(0,b)}\}^{2}=\mathbb{V}(\xi^{2})\leq\mathbb{E}(\xi^{4})
. It is easy to calculate the
density function of
F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(U^{(0,b)})
, which is

f_{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(U^{(0,b)})}(z)=\frac{f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}(z)}{b}I\left\{z\leq F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)\right\}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle f_{\xi}(z)=\frac{\phi(z)}{b}I\left\{-\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)}\leq z\leq\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi(z)}{\Phi\left\{\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)}\right\}-\Phi\left\{-\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)}\right\}}I\left\{-\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)}\leq z\leq\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)}\right\},

where
\phi(\cdot)
is the density function of standard normal distribution and

\Phi(\cdot)
is the c.d.f. of standard normal distribution.
Therefore, we know that
\xi
is distributed as a symmetric truncated normal
distribution. Due to Theorem 2.2 in
Barreto et al. 2024
, we have that

\xi
is sub-gaussian with a
variance proxy smaller than 1, which implies
\mathbb{E}(\xi^{4})\leq 16
. Applying (2)
in
Barreto et al. 2024
, we have that

\displaystyle\mu^{(0,b)}=\mathbb{E}(\xi^{2})=\mathbb{V}(\xi)=1-\frac{2\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)}\phi\left(\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)}\right)}{\Phi\left(\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)}\right)-\Phi\left(-\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)}\right)}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{2\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)}\exp\{-\frac{1}{2}F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(b)\}}{\sqrt{2\pi}b}=1-Q(b).

For
\sigma^{(0,b)}
, we have already proven that

\sigma^{(0,b)}\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\xi^{4})}\leq 4
. As for
X_{(r+1)}
, since

X_{i}\sim\chi_{1}^{2}
, it is easy to know that with probability at least

1-n^{-1}
, we have that
X_{(r+1)}\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}X_{i}\leq 4\log n
. Therefore, with probability at least

1-2e^{-\frac{\varsigma^{2}}{2}}-n^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{i=1}^{r}(1-X_{(i)})\right|\leq|Q(U_{(r+1)})|+\frac{4\varsigma}{\sqrt{r}}+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\log n}{3r},

and the bound in (
S.42
) holds since
Q(u)\geq 0,\forall u\geq 0
.
To prove
Q(u)
is decreasing and
Q(0)=1
,
Q(1)=0
, we start with some
properties of the following function:

\tilde{Q}(u)=\frac{2u\phi(u)}{\Phi(u)-\Phi(-u)}=\frac{ue^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2}}}{\int_{0}^{u}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}t},u\geq 0,

We have that

\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}(u)}{\mathrm{d}u}=\frac{e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2}}\{(1-u^{2})\int_{0}^{u}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}t-ue^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2}}\}}{\left(\int_{0}^{u}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}}.

Letting
g(u)=(1-u^{2})\int_{0}^{u}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}t-ue^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2}}
, we
have that
g(0)=0
and the derivative
g^{\prime}(u)=-2u\int 0^{u}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}t\leq 0,\forall u\geq 0
. Thus,
g(u)\leq g(0)=0,\forall u\geq 0
.
Therefore, we
have that
\tilde{Q}(u)
is decreasing. We also have that

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}(0)=\lim_{u\to 0}\frac{ue^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2}}}{\int_{0}^{u}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}t}=\lim_{u\to 0}(1-u^{2})=1\text{, and }\tilde{Q}(\infty)=\lim_{u\to\infty}2e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2}}=0,

which implies
Q(u)
is decreasing,
Q(0)=\tilde{Q}(0)=1
, and

Q(1)=\tilde{Q}(\infty)=0
, since
\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(u)}
is an increasing function,

\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(0)}=0
, and
\sqrt{F_{\chi_{1}^{2}}^{-1}(1)}=\infty
.

∎

Lemma S.9
.

Under Assumption
4
and assume that

\varepsilon\sim\mathbb{N}(0,\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})
and
\bm{x}\sim\mathbb{N}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma})
,

\forall\varsigma>0
,
0<c_{0}<8
, as long as
n_{\mathcal{T}}\gtrsim e^{2\varsigma}
,

r\leq\{1-(1+c_{0})p_{\gamma}\}n_{\Xi}
,
\gamma_{b}\gtrsim\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}
, we have that with
probability at least

1-Ce^{-\varsigma}-Ce^{-\{C(p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\wedge\log n_{\Xi}\}}-e^{-C\sqrt{r}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\varepsilon_{[k]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}\sqrt{\varsigma}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}Q\left\{\left(1-\frac{1}{\log n_{\Xi}}\right)\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right\}+\frac{\sqrt{\varsigma}\{(\varsigma^{2}\log n_{\Xi})\vee(p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\}}{\sqrt{r}}.

Proof.

For any
\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have that the joint distribution of

(\varepsilon,\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u},\varepsilon-\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}/\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}})
conditional on
\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}
is a
multivariate normal distribution, which is given as

\displaystyle f^{|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}}_{(\varepsilon,\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u},Z^{0})}(\bm{z}_{f})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{|\bm{\Sigma}_{f}|}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\bm{z}_{f}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{f}^{-1}\bm{z}_{f}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{|\bm{\Sigma}_{c}|}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\bm{z}_{c}-\bm{\mu}_{c}z_{3})^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{c}^{-1}(\bm{z}_{c}-\bm{\mu}_{c}z_{3})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{Z^{0}}}e^{-\frac{z_{3}^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle f^{|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}}_{(\varepsilon,\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u},|Z^{0}|=z)}(\bm{z}_{f})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{|\bm{\Sigma}_{c}|}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\bm{z}_{c}-s\bm{\mu}_{c}z)^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{c}^{-1}(\bm{z}_{c}-s\bm{\mu}_{c}z)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{Z^{0}}}e^{-\frac{(sz)^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{|\bm{\Sigma}_{c}|}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\bm{z}_{c}-s\bm{\mu}_{c}z_{3})^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{c}^{-1}(\bm{z}_{c}-s\bm{\mu}_{c}z_{3})}\right\}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{Z^{0}}}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}}.

which implies

\displaystyle f^{|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}},|Z^{0}|=z_{3}}_{(\varepsilon,\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u})}(\bm{z}_{f})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}}\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{|\bm{\Sigma}_{c}|}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\bm{z}_{c}-s\bm{\mu}_{c}z_{3})^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{c}^{-1}(\bm{z}_{c}-s\bm{\mu}_{c}z_{3})}.

Therefore, we know that conditional on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}
and
|Z^{0}|=z_{3}
, random vector

(\varepsilon,\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u})^{\top}
follows
\xi\bm{v}
, where
\xi

is a random variable that taking values
-1
and
1
with probability

\frac{1}{2}
and
\bm{v}
follows
\mathbb{N}(\bm{\mu}_{c}z_{3},\bm{\Sigma}_{c})
.
We have

\varepsilon\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u}=(\varepsilon,\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u})\bm{A}(\varepsilon,\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u})^{\top}=\xi\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{A}\xi\bm{v}=\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{v}
,
where

\displaystyle\bm{A}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\frac{1}{2}\\
\frac{1}{2}&0\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Thus,
\forall\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u}||Z^{0}|=z_{3},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}})=\mathbb{E}(\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{v})=trace(\bm{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{c})+\bm{\mu}_{c}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{c}\bm{\mu}_{c}z_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}-\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}z_{3}^{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}=\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}(\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}-z_{3}^{2})}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}:=m(z_{3})

and similarly,
\forall\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(\varepsilon\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u}||Z^{0}|=z_{3},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}})=\mathbb{V}(\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{v})=2trace(\bm{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{c}\bm{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{c})+4\bm{\mu}_{c}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{c}\bm{A}\bm{\mu}_{c}z_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{4}(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}})^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}+\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}-\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{4}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}+\left\{\frac{(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}})^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{6}}\left(\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}-2\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{4}\right)+\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{6}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{6}}\right\}z_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}+z_{3}^{2},

as long as
n_{\mathcal{T}}\geq\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{4}}
.
Applying Lemma 3.1 in
Bhattacharya 1984
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left\{\varepsilon_{[k]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]}^{\top}\bm{u}-m(|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])})\right\}\right|>A_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left\{\varepsilon_{[k]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]}^{\top}\bm{u}-m(|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])})\right\}\right|>A_{1}\Big||Z^{\gamma}|_{(k)},1\leq k\leq r,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left\{\varepsilon_{[i[k]]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i[k]]}^{\top}\bm{u}-m(|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])})\right\}\right|>A_{1}\Big||Z^{0}|_{(i[k])},1\leq k\leq r,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{r}\mathbb{V}\left(\varepsilon\tilde{\bm{x}}^{\top}\bm{u}\big||Z^{0}|=|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)}{A_{1}^{2}}\right\}=\frac{1}{\varsigma},

where

\displaystyle A_{1}=\sqrt{\varsigma\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left\{|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}^{2}+\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\right\}},

and the last inequality is due to Chebyshev’s inequality. We then bound the value

\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(1-\left|\frac{Z^{0}_{(i[k])}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}}\right|^{2}\right)\right|.

For any fixed
K_{\gamma}
, if
\gamma_{b}\geq|Z^{0}|_{(n_{\Xi})}-Z^{0}_{(1)}
, we know
that all the data points with nonzero
bias terms satisfy
|Z^{\gamma}|_{i}>|Z^{0}|_{(n_{\Xi})}
,
\forall i\in\{j:\gamma_{j}\neq 0\}
. In this case, it is equivalent to say that we put all the “contaminated”
points
|Z^{\gamma}|_{i}
,
i\in\{j:\gamma_{j}\neq 0\}
after the value

|Z^{0}|_{(n_{\Xi})}
. Therefore, as long as
r<n_{\Xi}-K_{\gamma}
, we know that for any
“clean” sample
|Z^{0}|_{(i)}
,
i=1,2,\ldots,r
, it is either be remained at the same
position or be put after
|Z^{0}|_{(n_{\Xi})}
. Note that under this circumstance, we
know that as long as
r<n_{\Xi}-K_{\gamma}
, the selected points are data points
with no bias terms. Therefore,
\forall k=1,2,\ldots,r
, we have that

|Z^{\gamma}|_{(k)}=|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}
. Using similar arguments as in Claim 3.3.1
in
Oliveira et al. 2025
, we can conclude that under the scenario we
considered,

|Z^{0}|_{(k)}\leq|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}=|Z^{\gamma}|_{(k)}\leq|Z^{0}|_{(k+K_{\gamma})}
,
\forall k=1,2,\ldots,r
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{\gamma}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{0}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{\gamma}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)\geq\sum_{k=K_{\gamma}+1}^{r+K_{\gamma}+1}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{\gamma}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)\geq\sum_{k=1}^{K_{\gamma}}\frac{|Z^{0}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}-K_{\gamma}+\sum_{k=1}^{r+K_{\gamma}+1}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{0}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq-K_{\gamma}+\sum_{k=1}^{r+K_{\gamma}+1}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{0}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right).

Thus, we know that

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(1-\left|\frac{Z^{0}_{(i[k])}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}}\right|^{2}\right)\right|\leq\max\left\{\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r+K_{\gamma}+1}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{0}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)\right|,\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{0}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)\right|\right\}+K_{\gamma}.

Now, since conditional on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}
,
\frac{|Z^{0}|^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\sim\chi_{1}^{2}
, we
know that, applying Lemma
S.8

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{0}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)\right|\geq A_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{0}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)\right|\geq A_{2}\Big|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{T}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1-2e^{-\frac{\varsigma^{2}}{2}}-n_{\Xi}^{-1},

where

\displaystyle A_{2}=Q(U_{(r+1)})+\frac{4\varsigma}{\sqrt{r}}+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\log n_{\Xi}}{3r}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{0}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)\right|\leq rQ(U_{(r+1)})+4\sqrt{r}\varsigma+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\log n_{\Xi}}{3}

Similarly, we have that with probability at least

1-2e^{-\frac{\varsigma^{2}}{2}}-n_{\Xi}^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r+K_{\gamma}+1}\left(1-\frac{|Z^{0}|_{(k)}^{2}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}}\right)\right|\leq(r+K_{\gamma}+1)Q(U_{(r+K_{\gamma}+1)})+4\sqrt{r+K_{\gamma}}\varsigma+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\log n_{\Xi}}{3}

\displaystyle\leq rQ(U_{(r+1)})+4\sqrt{r+K_{\gamma}+1}\varsigma+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\log n_{\Xi}}{3}+(K_{\gamma}+1)

\displaystyle\leq rQ(U_{(r+1)})+4\sqrt{r}\varsigma+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\log n_{\Xi}}{3}+(K_{\gamma}+1)+4\sqrt{K_{\gamma}+1}\varsigma

\displaystyle\leq rQ(U_{(r+1)})+4\sqrt{r}\varsigma+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\log n_{\Xi}}{3}+3(K_{\gamma}+1)+2\varsigma^{2},

where the second inequality is because
Q(u)
is decreasing and
Q(u)\leq 1
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(1-\left|\frac{Z^{0}_{(i[k])}}{\sigma_{Z^{0}}}\right|^{2}\right)\right|\leq rQ(U_{(r+1)})+4\sqrt{r}\varsigma+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\log n_{\Xi}}{3}+4K_{\gamma}+3+2\varsigma^{2}:=A_{3}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\varepsilon_{[k]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]}^{\top}\bm{u}\right|\leq\frac{1}{r}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left\{\varepsilon_{[k]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]}^{\top}\bm{u}-m(|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])})\right\}\right|+\frac{1}{r}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}m(|Z^{0}|_{i[k]})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left\{\varepsilon_{[k]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]}^{\top}\bm{u}-m(|Z^{0}|_{(i[k])})\right\}\right|+\frac{1}{r}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}(\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{2}-|Z^{0}_{(i[k])}|^{2})}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}\sigma_{Z^{0}}^{4}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r}A_{1}+\frac{1}{r}A_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{\varsigma}}{\sqrt{r}}\left\{2\sigma_{Z^{0}}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}+\sqrt{1+\frac{1}{\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}Q(U_{(r+1)})+\frac{4\varsigma\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{rn_{\mathcal{T}}}}+\frac{\varsigma^{2}\log n_{\Xi}\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{3r\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}+\frac{(4K_{\gamma}+3+2\varsigma^{2})\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{r\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}Q(U_{(r+1)})+\frac{\sqrt{\varsigma}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}}{\sqrt{r}}+\frac{\{\varsigma^{2}\log n_{\Xi}\vee K_{\gamma}\}\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}}{r\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}.

Remember that our bound above are derived under assumptions

n_{\mathcal{T}}\geq\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}
,
\gamma_{b}\geq|Z^{0}|_{(n_{\Xi})}-Z^{0}_{(1)}
and
r<n_{\Xi}-K_{\gamma}
. We consider the following
“good” set
\mathcal{A}
that satisfies

\displaystyle n_{\mathcal{T}}\geq\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}},\gamma_{b}\geq|Z^{0}|_{(n_{\Xi})}-Z^{0}_{(1)},r<n_{\Xi}-K_{\gamma}

\displaystyle\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\varsigma,K_{\gamma}\leq(1+c_{0})p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi},U_{(r+1)}\geq\frac{r}{2n_{\Xi}}.

Since
\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\varsigma
with probability at least

1-\frac{C}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}
, it is easy to prove
n_{\mathcal{T}}\geq\|\bm{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{2}^{2}/2\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}
with probability at least

1-\frac{C}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}
by taking
n_{\mathcal{T}}\geq C\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\varsigma/2\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}
. Similarly, since (
S.40
)
satisfies with probability at least
1-e^{-\frac{c_{0}^{2}p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi}}{8}}
, we also know that

r<n_{\Xi}-K_{\gamma}
with probability at least
1-e^{-\frac{c_{0}^{2}p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi}}{8}}
by taking

r\leq\{1-(1+c_{0})p_{\gamma}\}n_{\Xi}
. Since

Z^{0}_{i},i=1,2,\ldots,n_{\Xi}
are normally distributed, it is easy to know that

|Z^{0}|_{(n_{\Xi})}-Z^{0}_{(1)}\leq 4\sigma_{Z^{0}}\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}
with
probability at least
1-2n_{\Xi}^{-1}
. Therefore,
\gamma_{b}\geq|Z^{0}|_{(n_{\Xi})}-Z^{0}_{(1)}
with probability at least
1-2n_{\Xi}^{-1}
if

\gamma_{b}\gtrsim\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}
. Due to Lemma A.1.1
in
Oliveira et al. 2025
, we also know that
\forall\varsigma_{1}>0
, with
probability at least
1-e^{-2\varsigma_{1}}
, we have that

\displaystyle U_{(r+1)}\geq\frac{(\sqrt{r+1}-\sqrt{\varsigma_{1}})^{2}}{n_{\Xi}}.

Taking
\varsigma_{1}=\frac{r+1}{(2\log n_{\Xi})^{2}}
, we have that with probability at least

1-e^{-(r+1)/\{2(\log n_{\Xi})^{2}\}}
,

U_{(r+1)}\geq\left\{1+\frac{1}{4(\log n_{\Xi})^{2}}-\frac{1}{\log n_{\Xi}}\right\}\frac{r+1}{r}\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\geq\left(1-\frac{1}{\log n_{\Xi}}\right)\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}.

Therefore, with probability at least

1-\frac{C}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}-\frac{3}{n_{\Xi}}-e^{-\frac{c_{0}^{2}p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi}}{8}}-e^{-(r+1)/\{2(\log n_{\Xi})^{2}\}}-2e^{-\frac{\varsigma^{2}}{2}}-\varsigma^{-1}
,
we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{r}\varepsilon_{[k]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]}^{\top}\bm{u}\right|\lesssim\frac{\sigma_{\mathcal{T}}\sqrt{\varsigma}}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}Q\left\{\left(1-\frac{1}{\log n_{\Xi}}\right)\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right\}+\frac{\sqrt{\varsigma}\{(\varsigma^{2}\log n_{\Xi})\vee(p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\}}{\sqrt{r}}.

∎

Now, we prove Theorem
3
based on Lemma
S.6
,
Lemma
S.7
, and Lemma
S.9
.

Proof of Theorem
3
(I)
.

Remember that for target guided method the weighting matrix
\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}=\bm{I}_{n_{\Xi}}

and therefore, we know that according to (
S.27
), we know that

\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}
is the fix point of equation:

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}=\Theta\left\{(\bm{I}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top})\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}+\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}};\lambda\right\},

(S.43)

and
\hat{\bm{\beta}}=\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}+\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}})
, where

\Xi^{*}=\Xi_{\mathrm{tg}}^{*}
. Since

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}=\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}

\displaystyle=\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}(\bm{V}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}})\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}=(\bm{I}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top})\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}},

we have that

\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}=\Theta_{1}\left\{(\bm{I}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top})(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}})+\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}};\lambda\right\}.

Now, we prove that the all the eigen values of matrix
\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}
are in
the range of
[0,1]
. It is easy to observe that
\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}
is
non-negative and thus we only prove that the eigen values of

\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}
are less than 1. We know that, by definition, the eigen
values of
\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}
are the roots of

\displaystyle det\left\{\lambda\bm{I}_{n_{\Xi}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\right\}=\lambda^{n_{\Xi}-d}det\left\{\lambda\bm{I}_{d}-\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{n_{\Xi}-d}(\lambda-1)^{d-n_{\mathcal{T}}}det\left\{(\lambda-1)\bm{I}_{n_{\mathcal{T}}}+\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\right\},

which is strictly positive if
\lambda>0
and therefore, all the eigen values of
matrix
\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}
are in the range of
[0,1]
. This also implies that
all the eigen values of matrix
\bm{I}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}
are in the range of

[0,1]
and thus
\|\bm{I}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\|_{2}\leq 1
. Since

\displaystyle\|(\bm{I}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top})(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}})\|_{\infty}\leq\|\bm{I}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\|_{\infty}\|\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}\|\bm{I}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\|_{2}\|\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{d}\|\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{T}}\|_{\infty}=\sqrt{d}|Z^{\gamma}|_{(r)}.

Applying Lemma
S.6
and Corollary
3
, we know that
on
\Xi_{\mathrm{tg}}^{*}
,
\gamma_{[1]}=\gamma_{[2]}=\cdots=\gamma_{[r]}=0
and

|Z^{\gamma}|_{(r)}\lesssim\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}
with probability
at least

1-Crp_{\gamma}e^{\frac{-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}-Ce^{-\{(p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\wedge\log n_{\Xi}\}}
,
Due to the definition of
\Theta_{1}(\cdot)
, we know that if

\lambda>\frac{|Z^{\gamma}|_{(r)}}{\sqrt{d}}
, we have that
\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}=\bm{0}
is the fix
point of (
S.43
). Therefore, since
\lambda\gtrsim\sqrt{\log n_{\Xi}}
,
we know that
\lambda\gtrsim\frac{|Z^{\gamma}|_{(r)}}{\sqrt{d}}
with probability at
least

1-Crp_{\gamma}e^{\frac{-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}-Ce^{-\{(p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\wedge\log n_{\Xi}\}}

and

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}=\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}+\bm{V}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}})

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\bm{x}_{t}\bm{x}_{t}^{\top}+\sum_{k=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{[k]}\bm{x}_{[k]}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\bm{x}_{t}\bm{x}_{t}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{0}+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\varepsilon_{t}\bm{x}_{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{[k]}\bm{x}_{[k]}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{r}\varepsilon_{[k]}\bm{x}_{[k]}\right),

which implies

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{\beta}_{0}=\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top}+\sum_{k=1}^{r}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\varepsilon_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{r}\varepsilon_{[k]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[k]}\right).

Due to Lemma
S.1
and Lemma
S.7
, we know that with
probability at least

1-Crp_{\gamma}e^{-\frac{\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}-Ce^{-\{(p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\wedge\log n_{\Xi}\}}-2e^{-Cr^{1-\alpha_{3}}}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}
,
we have that

\bm{V}=\frac{1}{N}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top}+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i]}^{\top}\right)\geq\frac{1}{4},

where
N=n_{\mathcal{T}}+n_{\Xi}
. Now, combining Lemma
S.9
, we complete the proof.

∎

Proof of Theorem
3
(II)
.

Due to Lemma
S.6
, we have that with probability
at least
1-Crp_{\gamma}e^{\frac{-\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}-Ce^{-\{(p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\wedge\log n_{\Xi}\}}
,

\gamma_{[1]}=\gamma_{[2]}=\cdots=\gamma_{[r]}=0
.
From the proof of Theorem
2
(see Section
A.3.3
),
we can know that for
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathrm{tg}}-\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{\beta}_{0}=\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i]}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\varepsilon_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\varepsilon_{[i]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i]}\right).

Due to Lemma
S.1
and Lemma
S.7
, we know that with
probability at least

1-Crp_{\gamma}e^{-\frac{\gamma_{b}^{2}}{4\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}}}-Ce^{-\{(p_{\gamma}n_{\Xi})\wedge\log n_{\Xi}\}}-2e^{-Cr^{1-\alpha_{3}}}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{n_{\mathcal{T}}}}
,
we have that

\bm{V}_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{N_{f\lambda}}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{t}^{\top}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i]}\tilde{\bm{x}}_{[i]}^{\top}\right)\geq\frac{1}{4},

where
N_{f\lambda}=n_{\mathcal{T}}+c_{\lambda}\}r
and

c_{\lambda}=\lambda/(1+\lambda)
. Now, combining Lemma
S.9
, we
complete the proof.

∎

Proof of Corollary
2
.

Due to Proposition 4.1.1 in
Oliveira et al. 2025
, and the definition in of

Q(b)
in the proof of Lemma
S.8
, we have that
\forall b>0

\displaystyle Q(b)=1-\mu^{(0,b)}=|\mu^{(0,b)}-\mu|

\displaystyle=-\frac{\int_{0}^{1}\{F^{-1}(u)-\mu\}I(u<b)\mathrm{d}u}{1-b}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}(|X_{1}-\mu|^{q})\frac{(1-b)}{b}(1-b)^{-\frac{1}{q}}.

Therefore, the result holds by plugging in
b=(1-\frac{1}{\log n_{\Xi}})\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}
, which implies,

\displaystyle Q(b)\lesssim\frac{1-\left(1-\frac{1}{\log n_{\Xi}}\right)\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}}{\left(1-\frac{1}{\log n_{\Xi}}\right)\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}}\left\{1-\left(1-\frac{1}{\log n_{\Xi}}\right)\frac{r}{n_{\Xi}}\right\}^{-\frac{1}{q}}=\left(n_{\Xi}-r+\frac{r}{\log n_{\Xi}}\right)^{1-\frac{1}{q}}\frac{n_{\Xi}^{\frac{1}{q}}}{r-\frac{r}{\log n_{\Xi}}}.

∎

A.5
Calculation of degree of freedom in Section
5.2

For
\ell_{2}
penalty, denoting
\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}=\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}
,

\bm{V}_{\Xi^{*}}=\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}
,
c_{\lambda}=\lambda/(1+\lambda)
,

\bm{V}_{\lambda}=\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}+c_{\lambda}\bm{V}_{\Xi^{*}}
, we have that

\hat{\bm{\beta}}=\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{-1}\left(\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}+c_{\lambda}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}\right)
and

\hat{\bm{\gamma}}_{\Xi^{*}}=(1-c_{\lambda})(\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}-\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\hat{\bm{\beta}})
.
Therefore, We have that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\bm{y}}_{\mathcal{T}}\\
\hat{\bm{y}}_{\Xi^{*}}\\
\end{pmatrix}=\bm{M}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{y}_{\mathcal{T}}\\
\bm{y}_{\Xi^{*}}\\
\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\text{ where }\bm{M}=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}&c_{\lambda}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\\
c_{\lambda}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{X}_{\Xi}\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{-1}\bm{X}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\top}&c_{\lambda}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{-1}c_{\lambda}\bm{X}_{\Xi^{*}}^{\top}\bm{W}_{\Xi^{*}}+(1-c_{\lambda})\bm{I}_{n_{\Xi^{*}}}\end{pmatrix}.

There for the degree of freedom is defined as

\displaystyle\text{df}=\text{tr}\left(\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{-1}\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)+c_{\lambda}\text{tr}\left\{\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{-1}c_{\lambda}\bm{V}_{\Xi^{*}}\right\}+(1-c_{\lambda})\text{tr}(\bm{I}_{n_{\Xi^{*}}})

\displaystyle=(1-c_{\lambda})n_{\Xi^{*}}+c_{\lambda}d+(1-c_{\lambda})\text{tr}\left(\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{-1}\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}\right)

Since the trace of
\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{-1}\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}
equals the trace of

\left(\bm{I}_{d}+c_{\lambda}\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{\Xi^{*}}\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{-1}
,
denoting the eigen values of

\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{V}_{\Xi^{*}}^{-1}\bm{V}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\frac{1}{2}}
as
q_{i},i=1,...,d
, we
have

\displaystyle\text{df}=\text{tr}(\bm{M})

\displaystyle=(1-c_{\lambda})n_{\Xi}+c_{\lambda}d+(1-c_{\lambda})\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{1+c_{\lambda}\frac{1}{q_{i}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{n_{\Xi}}{1+\lambda}+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}d+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{q_{i}}{\lambda+(1+\lambda)q_{i}}.
